Cristian Chivu perde due attaccanti nel giro di poche ore. Prima la notizia dell'infortunio di Bonny che dovrà stare fuori 3-4 settimane, poi dal ritiro della Francia è arrivata quella su Thuram che ha lasciato il ritiro francese per fare ritorno in Italia. Senza contare un altro infortunio, quello di Aleksandar Stankovic. Secondo quanto è filtrato dal ritiro della Francia, il problema accusato da Thuram non dovrebbe essere particolarmente grave. "Ci sono buone possibilità, infatti, che si tratti di un semplice affaticamento al bicipite femorale della coscia destra. Meno rassicuranti, invece, le informazioni in merito a Stankovic", sottolinea il Corriere dello Sport.

"E’ ovvio che Chivu vivrà con un pizzico di apprensione le prossime ore. Questo pomeriggio ci sarà la ripresa degli allenamenti alla Pinetina, ma l’attesa sarà tutta per gli esami a cui verranno sottoposti, tra oggi e domani, Thuram e Stankovic. L’allarme suona soprattutto in attacco. Bonny, come premesso, è fuori causa fino all’inizio di dicembre. L’obiettivo è riaverlo a disposizione il 5, contro il Frosinone, oppure il 9, contro il Borussia Dortmund".

"Lautaro, invece, è fermo dal 13 ottobre, quando in occasione del match con il Bruges ha accusato un risentimento ai flessori della coscia sinistra. Il suo recupero è quasi ultimato. Verrà finalizzato proprio nel corso di questa pausa. Ma non ci sono assolute garanzie che possa essere subito disponibile alla ripresa contro l’Atalanta. Non è da escludere, insomma, che il suo rientro slitti alla sfida con lo Stoccarda del 25 novembre".

Dopo la vittoria col Como, non sono arrivate buone notizie per l'Inter dall'infermeria. Mentre Bonny non è partito per un problema muscolare, rientreranno invece Marcus Thuram e Aleksandar Stankovic.

"Non c’è stato il tempo materiale per applaudire il countdown di Lautaro, pronto a tornare in gruppo a partire da lunedì: l’Inter deve fronteggiare altri due infortuni in attacco. Uno sembra lieve e riguarda Marcus Thuram, visitato dallo staff medico della nazionale francese e rispedito subito a Milano. Oggi farà altri esami con lo staff guidato dal dottor Volpi ma le indiscrezioni a distanza parlano di un semplice affaticamento muscolare. Niente di così grave da pregiudicare la sua stagione: potrebbe mostrare il pollice alzato a Cristian Chivu già per la prossima partita contro l’Atalanta, il 22 novembre a Bergamo. È stata la classica “pizzicata” dietro alla coscia, una lesione nemmeno leggerissima ai flessori che lo estrometterà dalle partite per un mese. Niente nazionale anche per lui, che la Costa d’Avorio ha legittimato a non partire invano per il raduno", scrive La Gazzetta dello Sport.

Claude Meetings Joins the Call — Your Overlay Still Lives on Dual-Track WASAPI Capture

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Anthropic launched Claude Meetings on 10 November 2026. The feature adds Claude as a visible participant on Google Meet (beta for Team and Enterprise). While people are still talking it can draft the plan under discussion, sketch a timeline on a whiteboard, answer questions in the meeting chat, and pull data from connected apps. Notes and action items land in claude.ai after the call. It posts a notice when it joins and refuses external guests unless an admin has authorized them.

That is a clean product move for knowledge work. It is also orthogonal to the problem that actually bites when you need live language help on a Windows call or interview.

Claude Meetings joins the call. It receives the mixed audio stream the platform already has, plus any shared screen or chat. It does not sit on your local audio endpoints. It does not give you a second, private track of your own microphone that never enters the call. It does not let you paint a translation overlay that is invisible to screen share. And it does not run when you are on a platform it has not integrated with yet, or when you prefer not to invite a third-party participant at all.

For a developer who needs real-time speech-to-text and translation during a technical interview, a sales call, or a support session in another language, those constraints matter. The useful path is still local capture of two tracks, local or BYOK processing, and an overlay that never appears in the shared desktop.

Classic amplitude/frequency sketch. Real Windows capture graphs also include shared-mode mixing, communications enhancements, and device-specific jitter that no remote meeting participant can remove.

A single mixed stream loses speaker identity. For live translation you need:

That separation lets the STT engine produce clean transcripts for each side and the UI show them side-by-side without injecting a bot into the meeting.

On modern Windows the practical stack is:

A “sub-50 ms time-to-final” cloud model is real once it has clean PCM. It is almost irrelevant if the samples have already been delayed or dropped inside the audio engine. End-to-end partials on a good day land in the 80–150 ms range; Bluetooth or a busy shared-mode session pushes that higher.

Multiple waveforms. Dual-track capture keeps the mic and the remote side separate so each can be biased, timed, and translated independently.

Keep the audio callback under a few hundred microseconds. Do the format conversion, VAD, and network/model work on another thread. Pin the capture thread and the audio interrupt affinity if DPC spikes are eating your buffers (Process Lasso’s LatencyTest or raw ETW still surface those).

Claude Meetings is a useful new participant for teams that want the model inside the call. For the developer who needs the language barrier gone on their own machine, without joining a bot and without leaking the overlay, the work is still the same: open two WASAPI clients, keep the callback empty, align the tracks, and feed a tight STT/translation path. The model improvements keep arriving. The capture budget does not move until you measure and tighten it yourself.

I'm building Talkaside, a Windows app for job interviews and calls in another language. Drafted with AI assistance from this project's code and logs.

My IEnumerable Parameter Boxed 80 MB. PGO Clawed Back 85%.

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I had a helper that summed a column of ints. Tiny method, the kind you write without thinking. I'd typed its parameter as IEnumerable out of pure habit, the "accept the most general thing you can" reflex that every C# tutorial drills into you. Months later it turned up near the top of an allocation trace, and I stared at it for a while because there's no new anywhere in it. It's a foreach and a +=. What could it possibly be allocating?

The allocation wasn't in my code. It was hiding in the foreach.

Same body, character for character. The only thing that differs is the parameter type, and that one difference decides whether the enumerator the foreach grabs lives on the stack or on the heap.

Here's why. List.GetEnumerator() returns List.Enumerator, which is a struct. When you foreach over a concrete List, the compiler binds straight to that struct method and the enumerator never leaves the stack. But IEnumerable.GetEnumerator() returns IEnumerator, an interface. To pass a struct back through an interface reference, the runtime has to box it. So every call into the interface version allocates one boxed enumerator before the loop even starts.

I measured it the way I measure most of these: GC.GetAllocatedBytesForCurrentThread() wrapped around two million calls on an eight-element list. Allocation counts don't jitter the way timings do, so the number actually means something.

Zero versus twelve megabytes for identical arithmetic. Then I ran it again, expecting the same twelve.

A different number. Allocation counts are supposed to be deterministic. Same work in, same bytes out. A figure that wobbles between runs means something non-deterministic is deciding how much to allocate, and that's the clue that pointed me at the JIT.

.NET compiles your hot methods twice. The first pass, tier-0, is fast and dumb and boxes the enumerator on every single call. While that version runs, the JIT keeps notes: which concrete type actually shows up at this call site? Every time, it's List. So when the method gets hot enough to recompile at tier-1, the JIT bakes in a guard that says "if it's a List, run the inlined struct enumerator, skip the box," and the common path stops allocating. The ~12 MB I measured is just the boxes from the warmup window, before tier-1 took over. How many that is depends on how quickly the method tiered up on that particular run, which is exactly why the number wobbles.

If you want to see the box with no PGO covering for it, switch Dynamic PGO off:

There's the honest number. Forty bytes a call, two million calls, exactly 80 MB, flat across runs. That's the real weight of a boxed List.Enumerator, and PGO had been eating about 85% of it on my behalf without telling me.

The rescue comes with one condition: the call site has to be monomorphic. The JIT devirtualizes to one type precisely because it only ever sees one type. Hand the same method a mix and it can't pick a winner.

Back to boxing on nearly every call. Which means the methods most likely to pay full freight are the flexible, reusable ones that cheerfully accept whatever you throw at them. The exact spots where IEnumerable felt like the grown-up choice.

None of this touched gen0. Eighty megabytes didn't even trigger a collection on this box, because the container has a roomy ephemeral budget and best-of-a-few-runs is the lens I care about, not a lab-grade millisecond. For a method you call a few hundred times, 40 bytes each is noise. Type it however reads cleanest and move on. This only earns a second look on a genuinely hot inner loop, and on those, modern .NET already handles the monomorphic case for you.

My rule now, for whatever it's worth: when I own both sides of the call and the loop is hot, I type the parameter as the concrete collection, or I take a ReadOnlySpan and stop thinking about enumerators altogether. I keep IEnumerable for public APIs, where accepting anything is the whole point. "Accept the most general type" is great advice for a library and a quiet tax on a hot internal helper.

The thing I keep relearning is that the allocation wasn't in the code I wrote. It was in the contract I picked for one parameter, and whether the JIT argues me back out of it depends entirely on how predictable my call sites are.

Here's the full runnable sample. Drop it in a Program.cs, dotnet run -c Release, then run it again with DOTNET_TieredPGO=0 to watch the number jump to the un-rescued 40 bytes a call.

Have you caught one of these in your own hot paths, or is PGO covering for all of us quietly enough that nobody looks? I'd love to hear where it bit you.

— still counting bytes nobody asked me to count

What to Do With Your Lawn in November – 6 Quick Checks You Need to Make Now to Get Ready for Winter

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Fall yard cleanups tend to focus on weather-beaten perennials and messy plant skeletons lingering in beds and borders – but don’t neglect your backyard’s biggest plant!

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

For homeowners, November represents the last opportunity to prepare your backyard’s largest living feature for the rigors of winter. Yes, I’m talking about your lawn. The weather can change on a dime, and if you’re not careful, it can leave its mark all over your lawn. Depending on where you live, one week can mark the difference between lush grass and frozen turf.

Thankfully, these lawn care essentials are neither grueling nor time-consuming. Do these simple jobs now, and they will save you trouble and expense later. They will help to protect your turf over winter and give it the healthiest start next spring. From what to do with your mower to when to apply that last round of fertilizer, these pivotal lawn care tasks can help with any shape or size.

These tips can also help you whether you have warm or cool-season grasses to deal with. You just need to adapt the approach depending on which type you are growing. Cool-season grasses like Kentucky bluegrass, fescue, and perennial ryegrass remain semi-active in November. But warm-season varieties such as Bermudagrass, Zoysia, and St Augustine are approaching dormancy. Here are the 6 key tasks you need to check off before the first true hard freeze locks your soil for the season.

When freezing temperatures arrive, your lawn’s soil moisture solidifies, expanding underground and turning grass blades brittle. In severe cold snaps, winds can wick moisture, inducing winterkill. Conversely, heavy snow falling on debris-laden turf can create the stagnant breeding grounds favored by snow mold fungi. Keep your USDA hardiness zone and regional weather in mind with these tips, but try to think one step ahead of the worst-case scenario so you don’t get caught out.

Before diving into these tasks, it’s important to check your soil temperature. Turf reacts to ground conditions, with root growth in cool-season lawns tapering off once soil temperatures plunge below 45°F (7°C). Second, verify that your mower blades are sharp. Dull mower blades can shred dormant grass tips, leaving wounds that turn brown, lose moisture, and invite cold-weather fungal pathogens. If you haven’t got one, the Sharpal 103N All-In-One Lawn Mower Blade Sharpener from Amazon is a great-value gadget for restoring razor-sharp cutting edges.

Spending just an hour or so on late-fall lawn preparation right now will really pay off when the warm spring weather returns. It also gives your grass all the help it needs to get through the sleet, wind, frost and snow that could be waiting just round the corner. Whatever your growing zone and grass species, here’s what you need to do to get your grass fully winter-ready!

Allowing heavy leaves to carpet your turf over winter is one of the quickest ways to ruin a healthy sward. This is a vital task for all types of turf, whether your grass is going dormant for the winter or not. For grass that is still growing, thick layers of leaves block sunlight and energy production. For grasses in any stage of growth or dormancy, leaves can trap moisture, restrict airflow, and encourage disease. Left unchecked, smothering leaves will suffocate entire sections of turf, leaving bare, dead zones by spring.

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Your removal strategy should match the volume of fallen leaf cover. For light to moderate leaf scatter, use a mulching mower and let the nutrients fall naturally into the ground. However, if your yard is buried under dense, wet blankets from mature oaks or maples, rake or blow them off immediately. If you’re after a good rake, the True Temper Clog-Free 24-inch Poly Leaf Rake from Walmart is durable and reliable and won’t tear at late-season turf. Collect the leaves for rich leaf mold or vegetable garden mulch. Focus extra attention on perimeter fence lines and low dips in the lawn, where fall winds can quickly create suffocating drifts.

Timing your final mow comes down to knowing your grass type, and considering the weather. In northern regions, cool-season grasses (fescues, bluegrass, ryegrass) continue growing until ambient soil temperatures consistently sit below 40°F (4°C). So they could still be growing in November if the weather is mild, but plan for a last cut soon. In the south, warm-season lawns (Bermuda, St. Augustine, Zoysia) have ceased upward growth and could be dormant already, so your last cut may already have happened.

When making a final cut, aim for a medium length. Cutting grass too short will leave the lawn more vulnerable to stress over winter, exposing tender root crowns to frost heaving and biting winds. Conversely, leaving turf overly tall causes long blades to fold over under heavy snow or winter rain, forming dense mats that trap moisture and increase the risk of disease. Aim for a final height of 2-2.5 inches (5-6cm) for cool-season lawns, and 2 inches (5cm) for warm-season lawns.

Once complete, hose down the underside of the mower deck, empty the fuel tank or run the carburetor dry, and store your mower away clean. Buy Sta-Bil Storage Fuel Stabilizer Fluid from The Home Depot to help keep leftover fuel fresh while mowers are stored in winter and give effortless startups next spring.

Late-fall lawn fertilization can be either beneficial or destructive, depending on the species of grass you cultivate. For cool-season turf, late fall is often considered the most important feeding of the year. Cool-season roots remain active, absorbing nitrogen and potassium. So this last infusion of nutrients supports strong root development and helps encourage early spring green-up and drought resistance. Buy Scotts Turf Builder WinterGuard from Lowe’s for a slow-release boost to cool-season roots.

Timing depends on location. In some areas, November may be too late for a last application. Apply fertilizer only while the grass is still green and before the ground freezes solid. If you’re unsure, contact your local extension office for guidance. Meanwhile, warm-season grasses should not be fed in November. They are entering dormancy or are already dormant. Nutrients added now could trigger new growth that have zero cold tolerance, leaving the turf vulnerable to winterkill during sudden freezes. Wait until spring to feed your warm-season turf.

Cooler weather and shorter daylight hours reduce your lawn’s evaporation rates. Continuing to run summer lawn watering schedules through November wastes water and runs the risk of waterlogging. If your cool-season turf is green and receiving less than an inch of natural rainfall per week, you only need to provide occasional deep waterings. Irrigate only when the top two inches (2cm) of soil feel dry to the touch, and irrigate during morning hours so grass blades dry before nightfall.

Do think about winterization with watering, too. Trapped water in underground pipes, sprinkler bodies, and backflow preventers expands upon freezing, cracking brass valves and rupturing poly lines. Turn off the main water shutoff valve, deactivate the controller, and blow out the lines using a commercial air compressor, or open manual drain valves. Disconnect, drain, and coil all garden hoses, storing them indoors. Leaving a pressurized hose attached to an outdoor spigot traps water inside the sillcock, leading to burst interior pipes during winter freezes.

You can get Frost King FC1 Foam Faucet Covers from Walmart in double packs. Durable, weather-sealed expanded polystyrene shells with rubber cinch ties that insulate exterior spigots against freeze damage.

Spotting thin, bare patches in your lawn during November is frustrating, but rushing out with a bag of standard seed and fertilizer is usually a waste of time and money. Across most northern and transitional zones, soil temperatures have dropped below the 50-55°F (10-13°C) threshold required for grass seed germination. Seed spread now will absorb moisture, fail to sprout, rot in cold mud, or be devoured by birds. Similarly, warm-season varieties cannot establish from seed in late fall.

Use November to diagnose and prep. Determine why the patch developed: is it compacted soil from a high-traffic shortcut, poor drainage, pet urine, or heavy tree shade? Aerate the bald soil with a hand cultivator, incorporate a light dusting of compost, and make a plan for early spring overseeding. If you live in an area with reliable, long-lasting winter snow cover, you can experiment with dormant seeding. Spread cool-season seed over the prepped soil just before the first big snowfall. The seed will remain dormant over winter, and the freeze-thaw cycles work the seeds into the dirt for uniform germination during the first warm days of March.

Grass enters a vulnerable state in dormancy, and plants lose their natural elasticity. Walking, driving, or playing on frosted or frozen turf fractures brittle leaf blades and crushes delicate crowns. This creates winter lawn damage like marks and ruts that will not heal until active growth resumes in spring. Plan to keep people and pets off the grass for the next few months. Move playsets and heavy equipment off the lawn.

Another source of damage in winter is rock salt on nearby driveways and sidewalks. This can run off into lawn edges, drawing moisture away from grass roots and creating toxic soil conditions. So transition to plant-safe de-icing compounds. You can buy Natural Alternative Ice Melt from Amazon, which is safer for lawns and pet paws. Finally, avoid late-season core aeration or power dethatching, which can do more harm than good as grass prepares for winter.

Not every November lawn task will be required for every yard. It comes down to understanding your grass type, watching the weather forecast, and focusing on clearing leaves, timing a final cut, managing water correctly, and limiting traffic over vulnerable winter grass. Tackle these chores now to protect your grass over winter and to give it the best chance at a healthy, strong start next spring.

Cómo Crear Agentes de IA para la Operativa Empresarial del Día a Día: De los SOPs al Desarrollo con SDD

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 11 • Edited on Nov 11 • Originally published at datalaria.com

Existe una escena que se repite cada semana en cientos de comités de dirección y departamentos de operaciones a lo largo de 2026. Alguien proyecta su pantalla, abre un entorno visual o un cuaderno de código, conecta un modelo de lenguaje de última generación a una bandeja de entrada de correo y a una hoja de cálculo, escribe un prompt de diez líneas («Eres un asistente experto en operaciones, lee las facturas adjuntas y actualiza el inventario») y pulsa Ejecutar.

Durante los primeros noventa segundos, parece magia pura. El agente lee el PDF, razona en voz alta, extrae el importe y añade una fila perfecta a la base de datos. Aplausos en la sala. Se aprueba el despliegue para el lunes siguiente.

El miércoles a las 11:00 de la mañana, el director financiero recibe una alerta: el agente ha procesado por duplicado una factura rectificativa interpretando un abono negativo como un cargo positivo, ha sobreescrito el código de proveedor de tres pedidos urgentes porque el PDF venía escaneado en diagonal y, al encontrarse con un error 429 Too Many Requests en la API del ERP, ha entrado en un bucle infinito de reintentos que ha consumido 400 dólares en tokens y ha bloqueado la cuenta corporativa.

Bienvenido a la epidemia de los Vibe Agents: agentes construidos por intuición, sin contratos de datos ni fronteras de permisos, que deslumbran en una demostración controlada y colapsan al primer roce con la entropía del mundo empresarial real.

Sin embargo, cuando se diseñan con rigor, los agentes de IA son la palanca de productividad más poderosa de nuestra década. Lo vimos al analizar cómo HappyRobot ha alcanzado el estatus de unicornio automatizando llamadas logísticas reales, o cómo en Ingeniería S&OP V: El Cerebro Autónomo y Observabilidad e Inteligencia Operativa con Agentes los agentes transforman la cadena de suministro y la monitorización de operaciones.

La diferencia entre un juguete peligroso y un trabajador digital de alta fiabilidad no está en usar un modelo más grande, sino en el proceso formal con el que lo especificamos y gobernamos. Tras sentar las bases de ingeniería en SDD vs. Vibe Coding, en este artículo vamos a desglosar cómo crear agentes de IA simples y robustos para la operativa diaria de tu empresa, qué herramientas necesitas realmente hoy y cómo unir los clásicos SOPs (Standard Operating Procedures) con la metodología SDD (Spec-Driven Development).

Antes de hablar de metodologías teóricas, conviene abrir el capó de nuestra propia casa. Hace unos meses, en Datalaria nos propusimos un experimento radical de dogfooding: despedirnos de las tareas manuales de distribución, auditoría y gestión editorial construyendo el Proyecto Autopilot, una arquitectura multi-agente documentada paso a paso en nueve entregas (desde el planteamiento inicial de nuestra agencia autónoma y la configuración del cerebro con Gemini y CrewAI, hasta la lucha con las APIs corporativas, el despliegue en CI/CD con GitHub Actions y la auditoría automatizada con Copilot CLI).

En el Post-Mortem Técnico del Proyecto Autopilot confesamos sin filtros los tres golpes de realidad que todo ingeniero o responsable de operaciones sufre al llevar agentes a producción:

Aquella experiencia nos enseñó una regla de oro que hoy aplicamos a cualquier automatización corporativa: un agente empresarial eficaz no es un chatbot libre con acceso a una base de datos; es un microservicio con razonamiento acotado, envuelto en una camisa de fuerza determinista.

Cuando una empresa decide incorporar agentes de IA en su operativa cotidiana, el error más común es intentar construir desde el día uno un «Super-Agente General» capaz de responder a clientes, emitir facturas, gestionar el almacén y redactar contratos. Ese enfoque garantiza el fracaso.

La estrategia ganadora consiste en desplegar agentes de responsabilidad única (Single-Responsibility Agents), pequeños, simples y especializados en un único cuello de botella operativo. El 85% del retorno de inversión (ROI) inmediato en cualquier organización se concentra hoy en tres arquetipos fundamentales:

Observa qué tienen en común los tres arquetipos: ninguno de ellos requiere una interfaz conversacional abierta donde el usuario mantenga una charla filosófica con la máquina. Son agentes orientados a eventos (Event-Driven Agents): se despiertan ante un disparador (un webhook, un correo entrante, una fila nueva en una tabla o un reloj programado), ejecutan un trabajo cognitivo acotado y devuelven un resultado estructurado.

Aquí llegamos a la pregunta del millón: ¿Existe un proceso formal para diseñar estos agentes sin caer en el caos del 'vibe coding' y tenerlos bajo control estricto?

La respuesta es un rotundo sí, y nace de la convergencia de dos mundos que hasta ahora hablaban idiomas distintos: la Ingeniería de Operaciones clásica y la Ingeniería de Software moderna con IA.

En el mundo empresarial, cuando contratas a un analista de operaciones o de finanzas, no le entregas la contraseña del banco el primer día diciéndole «haz lo que veas conveniente». Le entregas un SOP (Standard Operating Procedure o Procedimiento Operativo Estándar): un documento normativo que detalla paso a paso qué debe hacer, qué comprobaciones debe realizar, qué sistemas puede consultar y a partir de qué importe debe pedir la firma de su director. Como vimos en De la Narrativa al Diagrama: Diseñando Procesos S&OP con IA y BPMN, las empresas llevan décadas perfeccionando la documentación de procesos.

Por otro lado, en la ingeniería de software, estamos viviendo el auge de SDD (Spec-Driven Development o Desarrollo Dirigido por Especificaciones), la metodología que analizamos a fondo en nuestro artículo hermano SDD vs. Vibe Coding. En SDD, el archivo fuente más importante de un proyecto ya no es el código en Python o TypeScript, sino la Especificación Formal Ejecutable (SPEC.md): un contrato escrito en Markdown estructurado y esquemas de tipos que define el comportamiento exacto del sistema antes de escribir una sola línea de implementación.

Cuando unimos ambos conceptos, obtenemos la Metodología SOP-to-SDD para Agentes Empresariales. En lugar de escribir prompts improvisados, cada agente de tu empresa nace de una ficha de especificación formal compuesta por cuatro pilares innegociables:

A nivel de ejecución en tiempo real, este diseño envuelve el cerebro probabilístico de la IA dentro de compuertas lógicas deterministas:

Figura 2: Pipeline de ejecución en tiempo real gobernado por especificaciones SDD y compuertas lógicas deterministas.

Una de las mayores barreras para crear agentes hoy no es la falta de herramientas, sino la parálisis por análisis ante la explosión de frameworks nuevos cada semana. Para construir agentes operativos bajo la metodología SDD sin volverte loco, solo necesitas elegir una pieza de cada una de las cuatro capas del stack moderno:

No uses un cañón atómico para matar una mosca. Como comprobamos en el Proyecto Autopilot, la arquitectura óptima combina dos tipos de modelos:

Aquí debes tomar una decisión honesta según el perfil técnico de tu equipo:

Hasta hace poco, conectar un agente a tu ERP, a PostgreSQL, a Google Drive y a HubSpot exigía escribir decenas de integraciones frágiles a medida. Hoy, como explicamos en nuestro análisis de MCP (Model Context Protocol): El Estándar que Quiere Ser el USB de la IA, puedes utilizar servidores MCP estandarizados. Tu especificación SDD simplemente declara qué servidor MCP tiene permitido consultar el agente y con qué credenciales de solo lectura o escritura limitada.

Un agente en producción sin telemetría es una bomba de relojería financiera y de seguridad. Necesitas dos recursos críticos:

Para ver cómo aterriza todo esto en la práctica real del día a día, construyamos la especificación y el núcleo de validación de uno de los agentes más rentables para cualquier empresa: el Agente de Conciliación de Facturas de Proveedor (AG-FIN-01).

Antes de abrir n8n o tocar una línea de Python, el responsable de operaciones y el ingeniero acuerdan este documento Markdown de una página en el repositorio:

Con la especificación aprobada, traducimos el contrato de salida y la matriz semafórica a código determinista con Pydantic. Fíjate en un detalle crucial de diseño: no le pedimos al LLM que decida si la factura va a la Zona Verde o Roja (porque los LLMs pueden equivocarse al comparar porcentajes); le pedimos al LLM que extraiga los datos estructurados, y es nuestro validador determinista el que aplica la ley financiera:

Este patrón arquitectónico de menos de 45 líneas elimina de raíz el 99% de los accidentes operativos:

A medida que nos acercamos al horizonte de la Inteligencia Artificial General (AGI), resulta tentador pensar que pronto no necesitaremos especificaciones, ni contratos de tipos, ni matrices semafóricas porque los agentes «simplemente entenderán lo que queremos».

Sin embargo, la historia de la lógica y la computación nos enseña exactamente lo contrario. Como exploramos en nuestro homenaje a Kurt Gödel y sus Teoremas de la Incompletitud, ningún sistema formal complejo puede demostrar su propia consistencia ni validar sus propios axiomas desde dentro. Un agente de IA, por brillante que sea su capacidad de razonamiento, opera siempre dentro de un marco de objetivos que necesita anclarse en reglas de negocio externas verificables por humanos.

En la empresa real, la autonomía sin especificación no es innovación: es negligencia operativa. La verdadera ventaja competitiva no pertenece a las organizaciones que lanzan decenas de Vibe Agents inconexos que nadie sabe cómo auditar, sino a aquellas que están transformando su conocimiento operativo en Especificaciones Ejecutables (SDD): contratos claros, versionados y deterministas donde humanos y agentes colaboran con total trazabilidad.

Este artículo completa los cimientos de nuestra arquitectura agéntica:

Tu Escuadrón de Ingeniería IA de 12 Agentes Especializados bajo Metodología SDD: Despliega en tu propia organización el mismo sistema operativo agéntico (12 roles: Orquestador con triaje determinista XS–XL, Product Analyst, Arquitecto de Sistemas, Diseñador UX/UI con design.md, Frontend, Backend, APIs & MCP, Media & i18n, Monetización, Seguridad, QA Verifier con derecho a veto y Release/DevOps) con el que construimos y auditamos las aplicaciones de Datalaria sin acumular deuda técnica.

Y en tu organización, ¿seguís confiando la operativa diaria a prompts improvisados que rezan para que el modelo no alucine, o habéis empezado ya a traducir vuestros procesos de negocio en especificaciones formales ejecutables?

Wazuh rule with <hostname> matches in wazuh-logtest but never fires for agent events

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 11 • Originally published at atkvn.com

For an event that comes from an agent, analysisd sets the host name to the agent name before rules run, so in a rule is compared with that name. The alert still shows the syslog host under predecoder.hostname, which is why the rule looks like it should have matched. wazuh-logtest treats a pasted line as a local event, keeps the syslog host, and matches, so the test passes and production does not.

All of this was measured on Wazuh 4.14.7 unless stated otherwise.

Run your line through wazuh-logtest the way analysisd sees an agent event, by passing an agent-style location. Use your agent's ID, name and IP, and the location the event comes from:

Paste the log line. If your rule matched with the default location (stdin) but not with this one, the host name condition is the cause.

Every event reaches analysisd with a location. A local event has a location like stdin or a file path. An event from an agent has one that starts with the agent ID, like [002] (cae) 192.168.8.28->journald. In the 4.14.7 source (src/analysisd/cleanevent.c, around lines 543-584), a location that starts with [ makes analysisd take the text between the parentheses, the agent name, as the event's host name. For a local event it keeps the host name read from the syslog header.

in a rule is compared with that value (rules.c, around lines 2875-2880), as an OS_Match expression by default. The predecoder.hostname in the alert JSON is parsed again from full_log when the alert is written (json_extended.c), so it shows the syslog host even when the rule compared against the agent name.

We checked it on a 4.14.7 manager with one su line whose syslog host is DN4, a parent rule reached on every run, and two children: one with DN4, one with ^cae$.

In all three runs logtest printed hostname: 'DN4' in its decoding phase. The value it prints is not the value the rule compares.

Without ^ and $, cae also matches an agent called caesar (see the third row above for the anchored rule not firing on caesar).

Know what it costs: if the agent is renamed, the rule stops matching and nothing tells you. Keep the agent name in the rule's description so a rename is easy to trace.

Test the way production runs: always pass the agent-style location to wazuh-logtest for rules meant for agent events.

is not a shortcut: reading the same source, for agent events it is compared with the whole (cae) 192.168.8.28->journald string, not the journald the alert shows. We have not measured it.

One release (4.14.7), one single-node throwaway manager, one su line, tested with wazuh-logtest and an agent-style location. We did not send a journald event through a real agent; the live behaviour matches a user report on wazuh/wazuh#39536, where a step stopped every event from an agent named cae on two nightly runs. was read from the source, not measured. Events that reach the manager over syslog () rather than from an agent are not covered here.

Wazuh 4.14.8 (released 25 September 2026) ships byte-identical source files for rule loading and matching (rules-config.c, rules.c, cleanevent.c, logmsg.h, list_op.c); we compared the files, we did not rerun the tests on 4.14.8.

Full write-up with the lab output: https://atkvn.com/fix-hostname-rule-never-fires-for-agent-events.html

VPS Hardening: 10 Practices That Reduce Risk

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A virtual private server (VPS) is easy to deploy and surprisingly easy to leave exposed. A developer provisions a Linux instance, installs Docker, configures a reverse proxy, and deploys an application. Everything works. Six months later, the server has accumulated open ports, unused accounts, outdated containers, and configuration changes nobody remembers making.

The problem is rarely one catastrophic mistake. It is the gradual loss of control over what runs on the server, who can access it, and how it can be recovered.

Effective VPS security comes down to three priorities: reduce what an attacker can reach, limit what compromised software can do, and make recovery predictable.

These practices focus on Ubuntu and similar Linux distributions hosting web applications, APIs, and containers.

A common VPS issue is configuration drift. Developers install packages, modify firewall rules, and adjust services directly on the server. Eventually, nobody can reproduce the original configuration.

Treat the VPS as something you should be able to replace.

Keep the initial configuration in Ansible, cloud-init, or another provisioning system. Record required packages, firewall rules, application users, and service configurations.

Separate persistent data from server configuration. Databases, uploaded files, and encryption keys need explicit backup and recovery procedures.

A practical test is to provision a second VPS and deploy the application without copying the original machine's filesystem. If that requires recovering undocumented commands from shell history, the infrastructure is not sufficiently reproducible.

This also improves incident response. When compromise is suspected, rebuilding from trusted images and restoring verified data is generally safer than attempting to remove every possible persistence mechanism.

2. Keep SSH Off the Public Internet When Possible

SSH keys are essential, but authentication alone does not eliminate the risks of exposing an administrative service.

For a VPS administered by a small team, consider making SSH accessible through a private network such as WireGuard or Tailscale. Alternatively, restrict inbound connections to known administrative IP addresses through the hosting provider's firewall.

If SSH must remain public, disable password authentication and direct root login after confirming that a separate administrator account works.

On Ubuntu, check both /etc/ssh/sshd_config and any included files under /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/. OpenSSH generally uses the first obtained value for each configuration keyword, so adding a setting at the end of the main file may not override an earlier one.

Validate the configuration with sshd -t and inspect effective settings with sshd -T. For conditional Match rules, use sshd -T -C with the relevant connection parameters.

Keep an existing SSH session open until a new administrative connection succeeds.

Protect developers' private SSH keys, preferably with hardware backing, and revoke credentials when people leave the team. Changing SSH from port 22 may reduce automated scanning noise, but it does not replace access restrictions.

Developers often configure a firewall once and assume the server remains protected. Meanwhile, a debugging service or database may begin listening on every network interface.

This displays listening TCP and UDP sockets and their associated processes. It helps you find unexpected listeners, but it does not show which services are externally reachable, especially when containers or network address translation are involved.

For a typical web application, public access may only be necessary on ports 80 and 443. Databases, message brokers, metrics endpoints, and administration interfaces should generally remain private.

Use two levels of enforcement: the provider's network firewall and a host firewall such as UFW or nftables. Remember that container networking may introduce additional firewall rules.

Also check IPv6. Restricting access through IPv4 rules does not guarantee that the same service is inaccessible over IPv6.

After modifying firewall rules, test connectivity from an external network. Checking what remote clients can actually reach is more reliable than inspecting firewall configuration alone.

4. Review Docker Port Publishing Carefully

Docker introduces a common security mistake: exposing services that developers intended to keep internal.

Consider this Docker Compose configuration:

With Docker's default port publishing behavior, this makes the application available through all host interfaces, potentially including public IPv4 and IPv6 addresses.

If a reverse proxy running directly on the host needs access, bind the published port to localhost:

On current Docker Engine versions using standard bridge networking, this restricts access through the published host port to localhost. However, Docker versions older than 28.0.0 had a vulnerability allowing neighboring hosts on the same Layer 2 segment to access ports published to localhost.

Custom Docker network settings also matter. Direct routing and alternative bridge gateway modes can permit access through container IP addresses, independently of the published host address.

If the reverse proxy also runs in Docker, connecting both services through an appropriate Docker network can eliminate the need to publish the application port on the host.

Another important detail: Docker's firewall rules can bypass restrictions that administrators expect UFW to enforce.

docker ps --format "table {{.Names}}\t{{.Ports}}"

Review unnecessary port mappings, privileged containers, Docker socket mounts, and access to host directories. Verify actual exposure externally rather than relying exclusively on firewall status or container listings.

5. Restrict What Compromised Applications Can Do

Even a well-configured firewall cannot prevent exploitation of vulnerabilities in applications that must remain publicly accessible.

The next question is what happens after an attacker controls an application process.

Run services under dedicated accounts without unnecessary permissions. A Node.js API, for example, generally should not need root privileges, write access to application code, or access to another application's configuration files.

For system services managed by systemd, inspect the available hardening restrictions:

This assumes the application data directory exists and has appropriate filesystem permissions.

These controls restrict privilege escalation and filesystem access. They do not provide complete process isolation. In particular, ProtectSystem=strict does not make the API filesystems /dev, /proc, and /sys read-only, and filesystem restrictions do not prevent communication through accessible Unix sockets.

Test the configuration against normal application behavior before deploying it to production. Some applications require additional writable directories or access to system resources.

For containers, follow the same principle: run processes as nonroot where practical, remove unnecessary Linux capabilities, and avoid privileged mode.

The goal is to give each service only the access required to perform its job.

Automatic Linux updates are necessary, but they do not cover every component running on the VPS.

Ubuntu's unattended-upgrades can install distribution security updates, but third-party repositories require appropriate configuration. Application dependencies, downloaded binaries, and container images need separate update processes.

Track three maintenance areas: the host operating system, supporting infrastructure such as Docker Engine, and application dependencies.

Rebuild container images regularly using maintained base images. Scan dependencies during development and deploy updated images through the normal release process.

Also plan restarts. Installing a patched library does not necessarily replace vulnerable code already loaded into memory.

On Ubuntu 24.04 LTS, the needrestart package, when installed and invoked through APT, automatically restarts affected services by default, subject to configured exclusions. This also applies to unattended upgrades and can unexpectedly interrupt production workloads.

Review its behavior and configure exclusions for critical services when necessary. Schedule their restarts during maintenance windows rather than leaving vulnerable code running indefinitely.

Kernel updates may require a reboot unless an appropriate live patching mechanism covers the vulnerability.

7. Stop Treating Environment Files as Secret Storage Solutions

Secrets frequently leak through deployment shortcuts. Developers copy .env files between servers, embed tokens in scripts, or accidentally expose credentials through logs.

Environment files can be an acceptable configuration mechanism, but they are not a dedicated security boundary.

Use a secrets manager where practical. For smaller deployments, restricted configuration files or systemd credentials can be reasonable alternatives.

Avoid placing secrets in command arguments, where they may appear in process information or operational logs. Environment variables are convenient, but they can also be exposed through application diagnostics, process inspection under appropriate privileges, or a compromised application.

Also check whether the hosting platform provides instance credentials or sensitive information through a metadata service. On applicable cloud platforms, a server-side request forgery (SSRF) vulnerability may allow attackers to access metadata that the application can reach.

Use the provider's metadata protections, restrict unnecessary access, and assign instance credentials only the permissions required.

Finally, make credential rotation routine. Replacing a leaked database password should not require rediscovering the entire deployment procedure.

8. Collect Logs That Help Investigate Incidents

A VPS may record authentication attempts and application errors, but local logs have limited value if attackers can erase them after gaining privileged access.

Forward important events to a separate logging destination. Prioritize authentication activity, privilege escalation, application errors, reverse proxy requests, and changes to important configurations.

Build alerts around events requiring attention. Repeated failed SSH logins are common on publicly accessible servers. A successful administrative login from an unexpected source is usually more useful to investigate.

Monitor disk consumption too. Container logs without appropriate rotation can exhaust available storage and disrupt healthy applications.

Configure log retention, protect sensitive information in log records, and make sure the logging system does not become another source of outages.

9. Make Backups Survive a Server Compromise

Provider snapshots are convenient, but they should not be the only recovery mechanism.

If an attacker compromises the hosting account, snapshots controlled by that account may also be exposed or deleted.

Maintain separate backups of databases, uploaded files, and essential configuration. Encrypt them and use storage with independent credentials. Where supported, enable retention controls that prevent backups from being immediately modified or deleted.

For databases, use backup methods that ensure consistency rather than assuming a simple filesystem copy is always sufficient.

Provision a clean VPS, restore recent data, start the application, and verify that it works. Check more than whether the restore command completed. Confirm that the application can read its data and perform important operations.

This reveals missing files, expired credentials, and undocumented dependencies before a real incident occurs.

VPS hardening deteriorates as developers add services and modify configurations. A short recurring audit is more useful than a lengthy checklist performed only during initial deployment.

Automate checks for unexpected open ports, outdated packages, expiring TLS certificates, failed backups, and important configuration changes.

A weekly review should answer four questions:

Run external checks where possible. Monitoring from another system can detect problems even when the VPS is unavailable or compromised.

Compare the deployed configuration against your provisioning files. Investigate unexpected differences rather than letting them become the new undocumented baseline.

Chi prendere e chi evitare al Fantacalcio del Sassuolo di D'Aversa

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Il Sassuolo ha esonerato Alberto Aquilani dopo cinque sconfitte consecutive e diciassettesimo posto in classifica in Serie A, un punto in più del Monza terzultimo. Al suo posto è arrivato Roberto D'Aversa con un contratto valido fino al 30 giugno 2027 con l'opzione per un'ulteriore stagione.

D'Aversa è un allenatore pragmatico, che adatta il modulo ai giocatori a disposizione, ma ha un'identità tattica abbastanza riconoscibile. Negli anni ha utilizzato diversi moduli a seconda delle squadre allenate e dei giocatori a disposizione in rosa. Lo scorso anno ha optato per la difesa a 3 ma nelle esperienze a Parma e Lecce ha utilizzato la difesa a 4.

Indipendentemente dal modulo utilizzato (3-4-2-1 o 4-3-3) fiducia in Berardi e Laurientè, due freccie nell'arco del nuoov allenatore: entrambi al di sotto delle aspettative in queste prime 11 giornate con il capitano neroverde che ha realizzato appena 2 reti e una fantamedia del 6,39 mentre Laurientè poco meglio con 2 gol e 1 assist con una fantamedia del 6.73. Da valutare la titolarità di Sebastiano Esposito, il migliore del tridente di Aquilani con 2 gol e 2 assist e una fantamedia del 7.57 ma la cui titolarità è minata da Bowie, più "idoneo" a ciò che richiede il tecnico neroverde alle sue punte centrali. In difesa si può puntare su Obrador, appena 1 partita da titolare con Aquilani ma che D'Aversa ha avuto lo scorso anno quando era l'allenatore del Torino. Infine Idzes, ritornato dall'infortunio che lo ha tenuto fuori 4 partite in questo iniziio di campionato.

Non ci sono certezze sul modulo che D'Aversa utilizzerà con il Sassuolo (3-4-2-1 o 4-3-3) ma non sono consigliati i centrali difrensivi Walukiewicz, Caleta-Car e Candè a rischio insufficienze e malus. Non danno garanzie nè Muric nè Turati che in ottica fantacalcio rischiano di prendere tanti malus rendendoli portieri non appetibili in ottica fantacalcio.

Agents Look Ahead as AI Keeps Advancing: ‘We Have the Privilege of Being in a Human Industry’

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Jbeau Lewis, Cristina Baxter, Robby Fraser, Jarred Arfa, Josh Mulder and Shirin Nury joined the conversation at the 2026 Billboard Live Music Summit.

Agents Jbeau Lewis of UTA, Cristina Baxter of THE•TEAM, Robby Fraser of WME, Jarred Arfa of Independent Artists Group, Josh Mulder of TBA and Shirin Nury of CAA joined the Billboard Live Music Summit at the 1 Hotel in West Hollywood, California, on Tuesday (Nov. 10). Explore Billboard See latest videos, charts and news

Moderated by Billboard senior editor Taylor Mims, the panel spanned how the middle class of touring has been getting squeezed in recent years, rising production costs, smart tour packaging, and how far in advance tours are being booked. Related Michael Sanft, Attorney Who Represented Keffe D in 2Pac Murder Trial, Dies at 54 Kenny MacPherson, Veteran Music Publisher and Big Deal Music Co-Founder, Dies at 71 Diddy Moved From New Jersey Prison to Similar Facility in Pennsylvania

Baxter spoke of how rising tour costs and larger production has created a fine line on becoming profitable, with artists wanting to present their all but needing to be mindful of budget. For artists performing clubs and theaters, those challenges are even more apparent.

“The margins are just so razor thin now,” Baxter says. “Whether a tour is profitable or not and becomes a successful, sold-out tour, can come down to just a couple of decisions, such as the number of days and the cost of a certain element in production.”

Arfa added, “There are situations when making money may not even be the goal. Maybe an artist is trying to ascend to a new level, and the conversations are how to break even.”

As ticket prices continue to rise, those on the panel also noted that concerts may be considered a more luxury experience. Following the COVID pandemic, touring experienced a boom with more artists on the road and more tickets sold than ever before. Now, those in the live industry are seeing that growth has begun to slow, with 2024 and 2025 experiencing a plateau in ticket gross, according to Billboard’s 2025 year-end touring report.

“There’s been a sea-change in our business post pandemic,” Lewis said. “We saw a dramatic rise and change in the model and behavior of consumers. Expecting it to rise consistently is unrealistic. I think we’re probably there now.”

Despite this, tours are being booked further in advance, with some agents already looking to 2028 and 2029. As they look ahead, agents note the need for flexibility.

“You need to be ready to route and reroute many times,” Baxter says.

Nury added, “I think we’re living in such an interesting time, with so much volatility in the label system. I think the rest of the industry is really looking to agents to act as the architects for a rollout.”

Lewis noted, “We’re going to keep booking ahead as far as we can, but it’s going to come with challenges.”

While looking to the future, the agents suggested that technological advancements and artificial intelligence don’t seem to pose a massive threat to their careers.

“AI pulls from a certain data set that’s available to it to make decisions, but there’s so much more that goes into the way a tour is routed and the reason you choose a timeframe,” Baxter said. “A lot of that is not publicly available information… I don’t see it impacting [touring and booking] the same way as some other industries.”

Nury concluded, “We have the great privilege of being in a human industry, a social industry.”

LIVE – Bitcoin Price Cooked? Gensler 2.0 Fears as Fed Blocks Crypto Bank

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Bitcoin is struggling to find its footing after another brutal sell-off pushed its price below $73,000. The decline wiped out hundreds of millions of dollars in leveraged positions, leaving us questioning if the market has entered a deeper correction phase.

Bitcoin is now trading around $73,600 after briefly losing the $73,000 level. Ethereum slipped below $2,100, while XRP approached $1.05 and Solana struggled to hold $82. More than $723 million in crypto positions were liquidated over 24 hours, with nearly 79% coming from long bets.

The timing is particularly uncomfortable for Bitcoin bulls. October’s hotter-than-expected US inflation report has revived concerns about interest rates, while recent US election results have introduced another layer of regulatory uncertainty. Now, a banking dispute involving the Federal Reserve is adding fuel to the fire.

The sell-off has several forces working against it. First, October’s US Consumer Price Index (CPI) increased 0.4% month over month, exceeding economists’ expectations of 0.3%. Core inflation also climbed 0.3%, above the 0.2% forecast.

The interest-rate outlook has become even more concerning. According to CME’s latest rate probability data, the chance of the Federal Reserve keeping its target rate unchanged at 3.75%–4% has fallen to zero. This represents a sharp reversal from just one day earlier, when the probability of unchanged rates stood at 16%. It was 17.3% a week ago and 15.9% one month earlier.

Markets now assign an 86.3% probability to a 25-basis-point rate increase, taking the target range to 4%–4.25%. Another 13.7% probability is assigned to a larger 50-basis-point increase.

The liquidation data paints an equally uncomfortable picture. CoinGlass recorded $732 million in forced closures across crypto markets over 24 hours on November 11. Long positions accounted for $578 million, meaning traders betting on higher prices absorbed most of the damage.

Bitcoin also lost its 50-week moving average near $76,100, ending a seven-week streak above the technical level. The breakdown followed a week of heavy institutional selling, with US spot Bitcoin ETFs recording approximately $1.28 billion in net outflows.

The combination of ETF withdrawals, leveraged liquidations and inflation uncertainty has created a difficult environment for buyers. Social chatter has pushed back against describing the move as a full-blown crash, pointing to comparisons with the Nasdaq’s monthly decline. Nevertheless, losing important technical levels has given bears another reason to test market confidence.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

Bitcoin price problems are unfolding alongside renewed political anxiety. Recent US midterm election results have strengthened Democratic influence in Congress, raising questions about the future direction of crypto regulation.

The crypto community is increasingly discussing the possibility of a Gensler 2.0 regulatory environment. The concern centers on whether a tougher approach could return for digital asset companies, particularly decentralized autonomous organizations (DAOs), digital asset treasuries (DATs), memecoins, and privacy-focused crypto.

JUST IN: 🇺🇸 CFTC Chair says they will "future-proof" crypto regulations."We can't allow Gary Gensler 2.0 to come in and tear it all up." pic.twitter.com/ik6gt3Xtns

The regulatory uncertainty could complicate efforts to establish clearer rules for the industry. While tokenization and stablecoin initiatives may continue under existing frameworks, Democratic oversight could slow new legislation and challenge the durability of regulatory guidance.

Meanwhile, Old Glory Bank CEO Mike Ring has accused the Federal Reserve of blocking his institution’s crypto-integrated banking launch at the last minute.

DeFi. The next level of financial freedom. Fly above the Fed! The blockchain is the runway.Hear CEO Mike Ring explain why. pic.twitter.com/AGlN7sU0Ou

Ring’s complaint, summarized in his public comments, shows the central bank remains hostile toward crypto-friendly banking initiatives. He described the setback as evidence that the Fed hates crypto.

Old Glory Bank is reportedly pursuing an alternative launch strategy that avoids requiring the same Federal Reserve approval, potentially targeting the first quarter of 2027.

What do all these mean for crypto? The immediate challenge for Bitcoin price remains getting back above $75,000 and rebuilding confidence after the latest liquidation wave. A deeper decline would put additional pressure on already nervous traders.

Yet the longer-term Bitcoin thesis has not necessarily disappeared. Concerns about government spending, monetary debasement, and currency purchasing power continue to support the argument for holding scarce digital assets.

Lighthouse issues urgent Ethereum node patch due to hidden security flaws

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ethereum's Lighthouse urged all beacon node operators to install a high-priority security patch ahead of a planned vulnerability disclosure.

Ethereum consensus client Lighthouse released a security patch on Nov. 9 and urged operators of all its beacon nodes to upgrade as soon as possible.

The v8.3.3 release makes that work a high priority for users who stake and those who do not, but the release notice rates validator-client upgrades as low priority for staking users.

As a consensus client, Lighthouse connects with other clients in Ethereum's proof-of-stake network. Its beacon node works with an execution engine to validate transactions in blocks, while the validator client connects to the beacon node and manages keys, according to the Lighthouse Book.

Operators can also run a beacon node without a validator client. Lighthouse's documentation includes data analysis and applications among these non-staking uses, and those operators receive the same high-priority beacon-node instruction as staking users in the v8.3.3 update table.

The project's priority definitions put high-priority work on a timescale of hours or days, to be completed as soon as possible. Low-priority work belongs in the next routine update cycle, with two weeks given as an example.

The beacon-node upgrade is high priority with or without staking. Staking users have a separate, lower-priority validator-client update.

Upgrade the beacon node promptly. The validator-client update has low priority and belongs in the routine cycle.

Beacon nodes used for applications or data analysis have the same high-priority instruction. No validator-client priority applies to this group.

Staking and non-staking beacon nodes are high priority; staking validator clients can use the routine update cycle.

Vulnerability mechanics and potential impact remain undisclosed. High priority describes upgrade urgency. Hours or days and two weeks are illustrative category guidance, not fixed deadlines. No advisory publication date is given.

Map the release's current priority table to operator roles. Hours or days and the routine-cycle example come from Lighthouse's general definitions. Connectors show editorial sequence or component priorities, not network traffic; positions are not a time scale.

Vulnerability mechanics and potential impact remain undisclosed. High priority describes upgrade urgency. Hours or days and two weeks are illustrative category guidance, not fixed deadlines. No advisory publication date is given.

Maintainers say v8.3.3 introduces no breaking changes, but still direct users upgrading from v8.2.x or earlier to read the v8.3.0 release notes, an additional consideration for operators who have skipped intervening versions.

The release says technical details of the vulnerabilities will be disclosed later. Links to security advisories are due to be added once network operators have had time to upgrade.

That approach follows Sigma Prime's Nov. 5 explanation of its disclosure process. The Lighthouse developer says it initially holds back vulnerability information to give users time to update their nodes, then adds details to release notes and GitHub Security Advisories.

The v8.3.3 notice does not report active exploitation or identify losses, and attack mechanics and potential impact remain undisclosed. The high-priority label does not disclose the technical severity or consequences of an attack.

The request is specific to Lighthouse's beacon-node software. For operators running both Lighthouse components, completing a validator-client update alone would still leave the beacon-node instruction outstanding.

Checking which component is being upgraded is key because the two entries in the release table have different schedules. The withheld details also leave claims of theft or an imminent chain halt unsupported by this notice.

InfoQ Homepage News Angular's TypeScript 7-Powered Compiler Enters Beta with Rust-Based Preprocessor

Nov 11, 2026 2 min read

The Angular team has shared details of a new version of its compiler, the Angular Preprocessor (ngp), which rewrites the analysis stage of the toolchain in Rust to clear the way for TypeScript 7 adoption. Alex Rickabaugh and Mark Techson announced the work on the Angular team blog in late September, shortly after Microsoft shipped TypeScript 7.0, whose Go-based rewrite removed the ts.Transformer API that Angular's ahead-of-time compiler depends on.

The existing compiler, ngc, wraps tsc and uses the Transformer API to turn decorators such as @Component and @Pipe into runtime instructions. The Go port offers no cross-language equivalent, and crossing the JS/Go boundary at every file would be prohibitive. The Angular team's answer is to decouple compilation from TypeScript's APIs and rebuild it on Oxc, the VoidZero Rust toolchain already powering Vite. The team pointed to the plan on X, framing it as a partial rewrite rather than a fresh start.

ngp runs in three stages. A Rust frontend using Oxc analyses files in parallel to discover Angular declarations and resolve metadata. The existing TypeScript template compiler then emits two artefacts, a runtime file containing the transformed component and a .ngtypecheck.ts file holding the Type Check Block (TCB) for template type-checking. Finally a downstream tool, whether tsgo, tsrs, esbuild or Rolldown, handles type-checking and JavaScript emission independently.

The bulk of ngp landed on 8 October in a single commit of more than 370,000 lines, around 56,000 of them Rust. Cédric Exbrayat of Ninja Squad benchmarked the beta against three projects ranging from 15 to 1,714 components and measured a 2x to 3x speedup on cold builds, with memory use on the largest project falling from 2.8 GB to 1.4 GB. Pairing ngp with tsrs and Rolldown gave the fastest pipeline, with tsgo and esbuild only marginally slower. They concluded there is further gains to be made:

This is already very promising, and there are certainly further gains to be made when the team focuses on performance optimization.

Christopher Holder at push-based.io took a different tack, writing vite-plugin-ngp to compile Angular files on demand. On the Super Productivity codebase (252 components), the dev server reached the first page in 10.5 seconds against 17.4 for ng serve, with template edits applying in 112 milliseconds rather than a 2.6 second full reload. On a generated 5,000-component app the gap widened to 1.6 seconds against 28.4. Holder cautioned that the plugin "is a prototype on a prototype" pinned to 22.3.0-next.2.

On Reddit, a number of developers are excited about the update and write up, with one commenting:

Read the whole article. Impressive. The Angular team surpassed my expectations!

VoidZero's own experimental Oxc Angular compiler, covered by InfoQ in April, reported a roughly 97% improvement on narrower workloads but is now described as "actively seeking maintainers". The Angular team confirmed VoidZero's work influenced the architecture and that the two groups have been collaborating, with ngp becoming the official path.

Teams on earlier Angular versions should first run the standard update guide to reach v20 or later. Adoption of ngp is expected to land in a minor release of Angular 22 once TypeScript 7.1 restores the compiler API; until then production builds should stay on ngc.

Angular is Google's open-source, TypeScript-first web framework, widely adopted across enterprise applications and built around standalone components and signal-based reactivity.

Presented by: David Iparraguirre - Senior Product Marketing Manager at Datadog, and Jessica Yang - Product Manager at Datadog

Il Milan vuole Tresoldi a gennaio: Cardinale insiste, il prezzo lievita

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Il Milan vuole Nicolò Tresoldi già nel mese di gennaio. Questa è la notizia che stamattina viene ripresa da tutti i principali quotidiani sportivi e che, in generale, è un po' nell'aria da qualche tempo. L'obiettivo di Gerry Cardinale, proprietario del Milan, è quello di intervenire subito sul mercato per poter elevare la qualità della rosa, aumentando così la possibilità di raggiungere l'obiettivo minimo stagionale a fine anno: la qualificazione in Champions League, cruciale da un punto di vista anche economico.

Questa mattina è in particolare la Gazzetta dello Sport che parla in maniera aperta dell'interesse del Milan per Nicolò Tresoldi già da gennaio. Nell'ultima estate il club rossonero ha realizzato l'acquisto più oneroso della sua storia, spendendo 75 milioni per Gonçalo Ramos. Il centravanti portoghese, a oggi, è fermo a un bottino di 3 gol e 2 assist. La decisione di andare su Tresoldi già da gennaio non vuole sconfessare l'acquisto di Ramos, anzi vuole cercare di valorizzarlo: si tratta di un'alternativa pronta ma soprattutto di un giocatore che potrebbe fornire anche l'opzione dell'attacco a 2. Quest'anno Tresoldi ha già segnato 13 gol in 15 partite, per questo il Milan vorrebbe anticipare la concorrenza ed evitare aste.

A insistere sul colpo Nicolò Tresoldi sarebbe proprio Gerry Cardinale che, secondo la rosea, ha ordinato al suo team mercato di affondare il colpo già a gennaio. Il proprietario rossonero è stato folgorato dalla tripletta dell'attaccante italo-tedesco all'Inter. Il problema è che le prestazioni di Tresoldi hanno fatto lievitare il prezzo del cartellino che oggi si attesta attorno ai 50 milioni. Dopo averne spesi 75 in estate, il Milan è pronto a un'altra spesa di questo tipo? Per Cardinale non ci sarebbero problemi: l'ammanco della mancata qualificazione in Champions League dell'anno scorso si può ricucire solamente spendendo con investimenti sicuri. Tresoldi è considerato come tale. Con il Club Brugge c'è già un canale aperto: e se dovessero sparare alto a gennaio, in estate il Milan tornerà alla carica.

The recent QPR development that Leeds United fans will find amusing

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Glen Kamara scored the winner for QPR against Lincoln City on Saturday, a sentence that has very rarely been said throughout the Finnish midfielder's career.

His lovely left-footed effort was his first club goal since January 2023, when he netted against St Johnstone for Rangers. QPR is the fourth club Kamara has played for since that goal, and his second in England.

Indeed, the 31-year-old spent a solitary year at Leeds United during their 2023/24 run to the play-off final under Daniel Farke.

He quickly became an important player for the Whites, starting 33 times and starting in all three play-off games, too, but was sold the following summer to Stade Rennais, his current parent club, during this loan spell at Loftus Road.

Kamara is quickly becoming a well-liked character in the R's fold, but his goalscoring ventures at the weekend was something that not even they could see coming when they secured his services for the year.

Kamara isn't exactly a midfielder famed for his ability to crash the box. The 31-year-old is a cultured veteran who is very good at keeping the play ticking and winning duels at a high rate.

And, ultimately, in a Leeds side that possessed the attacking talents of Crysencio Summerville, Georginio Rutter, Wilfried Gnonto, Dan James and Joel Piroe, he didn't need to be relied on to score goals as the number eight in Daniel Farke's side back in 2023/24.

So much so that, in 2,709 minutes of Championship football during his sole campaign at Elland Road, Kamara took just five shots. That's an average of one shot every 541 minutes, or just over six games per shot.

Even with all that quality ahead of him, that's still quite the record, and it became almost a running joke amongst Leeds fans that Kamara was almost allergic to letting one fly from the edge of the area, given how often he liked to move the play on.

For those Leeds fans who still keep an eye on what's happening in the Championship while they currently sit pretty in the top half of the Premier League, seeing Kamara's name on the scoresheet for QPR would have been quite a surprise.

What would have caught them even more off-guard, however, was the ability he showed to take the ball down with his back to goal, turn with his first touch, and curl one into the far corner with his weaker left foot.

Were you surprised by Glen Kamara's effort to secure the win against Lincoln City? Let us know in the comments.

Those who watched Kamara at Elland Road wouldn't know he had that in his locker, and they'd rightly be questioning where that was during his year in West Yorkshire.

The Finnish international has shown a little more attacking intent in a Hoops shirt, albeit not enough to see him top the goalscoring charts this year. His eight shots and three on target are already more than he managed in the entire 2023/24 campaign.

With finishes like that, he might be urged to take more shots as the season progresses.

It's taken a little while for Julien Stephan to settle on his starting midfield pivot this year, but it seems like Kamara is becoming the first-choice option there, whether that be next to Nicolas Madsen or Jonathan Varane.

The Dane's injury is making things a little easier at the moment, but as and when he returns, you get the feeling that it'll be Varane who makes way, rather than the 31-year-old.

Through AI, we'll rank the top 25 most intimidating football stadiums in British football, but which stadium comes out on top?

QPR are still searching for a little bit of consistency as we approach the November international break. They've put in performances which showcase why they are where they are, but a few batterings at the hands of Birmingham City and Southampton still create some hesitance surrounding their play-off ambitions.

However, they currently occupy that final position in the top eight, and if the Hoops can stay relatively injury-free over the next six months, they could well maintain that.

If they do stay in the top eight, Kamara will no doubt be playing a big part, and perhaps popping up with one or two more unlikely goals.
