Top 9 friendliest EFL Championship fanbases named and ranked by AI

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It's always nice to have a good reputation. In the Championship, there are some places that fans enjoy going to a little more than others.

That could be due to the size of the away end or where it's situated in the ground itself, the city or town you get to visit, or even the ability to have a beer or two with someone who supports the home team without feeling threatened in any way.

It doesn't just stop at an away-day experience, though. A community can be created within the home section, and the growth of a fanbase can often be put down to how welcoming and friendly the current supporters are, to the point where even neutrals can attend games and feel that they are part of something.

Earlier this month, Football League World asked AI to rank the nine toughest Championship fanbases. While this isn't the complete opposite, these fans are, per AI's admission, the friendliest in the second tier.

Evidently, it's not the opposite, as Derby County feature in both the toughest Championship fanbases and, indeed, open up the ranking on the friendliest list, too.

The Rams have a loyal following, but they've experienced heartache in the recent past, which helped bring everyone together. Derby owner David Clowes, a lifelong supporter who saved the club from administration after Mel Morris departed, stated that they were a week away from going bust, which must be incredibly sobering to hear as fans.

That's created a sense of community among the supporter base at Pride Park, and it's led to away fans being accepted a little more in the city than they perhaps were beforehand.

Plus, their recent win over Bolton Wanderers aside, away days have been pretty fun for travelling fans this season, so they've enjoyed their visit to Derby for that reason, too.

There's a theme brewing already in the infancy of this ranking, as Charlton Athletic have also undergone some ownership troubles which have united the fanbase and created even more of a community amongst them.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top nine toughest fanbases in the EFL Championship right now from least to most

The Addicks weren't close to going bust by any means, but they've had five different owners in six years, and that's made even more alarming by the fact that the GFP group have been in charge since 2023.

Additionally, Charlton are one of the few clubs in this division that know not to expect much in terms of a promotion or play-off charge this season. That can make their fanbase a little less reactionary after a defeat and a bit less bitter toward opposition fans after a game.

That being said, they're still passionate, and that creates a mutual respect between certain fanbases and a subsequent place on the list.

You can often find that supporter groups of London-based clubs who aren't the traditional giants of the game have a bit more of a community-based following.

QPR, like others, don't attract loads of tourists each game, and with the tight atmosphere at Loftus Road, you get the feeling that almost everyone in each row knows each other and connects with them, no matter what walk of life they're from.

They, too, have experienced the highest highs and the more frequent lows that a Championship side has to offer, and despite not being in the Premier League for over a decade now, attendances and support for the club haven't waned.

The community is there to see, and it creates a friendly atmosphere that some other London clubs simply don't have.

Lincoln City fans are enjoying the ride, with this their first second-tier campaign since the 1960/61 term. It's been made even better given the fact that they were in the National League just a decade ago.

The Imps are heading to grounds and welcoming clubs to Sincil Bank that they could only dream of competing with a lifetime ago, and that rise from obscurity to the Championship and the underdog mentality surrounding them have given the club plenty of neutral support.

And it's that mentality which makes them a relatively popular fanbase. Like Charlton, their primary goal is survival, so even if they lose 6-0 against West Ham United at home with nine men, things don't sour, and the back-and-forth banter with Hammers fans during that game just showcases why they're on this list.

Portsmouth have a passionate fanbase; there's no doubt about that, but AI believes that they're among the friendliest, too.

The South Coast side has to welcome away fans who have travelled plenty far to get there, and they make sure that the experience is as hospitable as it can be, albeit hopefully with a result which sends them home far from happy.

The away end at Fratton Park is one of the more fun ones to watch on TV, with the entire Milton End available for away supporters, and when that's packed, alongside a close-to-capacity home section, it makes for an entertaining game.

Pompey have also gone through the wringer financially and are working their way back to their former glory. That history of the fanbase banding together to help the club through difficult times is enough for AI to put them in the top five of this list.

As of writing, the atmosphere around Bristol City right now is incredibly toxic, with head coach Michael Skubala under some pressure after a 5-0 loss at Ashton Gate against Norwich City to lead into the international break.

The Canaries more than enjoyed their trip to Bristol because of that, but the Robins fans are well-renowned for being hospitable, while also providing passion and a desire to see the team improve.

Perhaps that run to the play-offs under Liam Manning in 2024/25 has created some extra expectation, and Bristol City fans may not be as welcoming to an opposition fan trying to engage with them after a defeat, but for now, they rank highly in AI's estimation.

Per the fan engagement index from last season, Swansea City were the highest-ranked Championship side, meaning that the fanbase in South Wales is one of the most content with the state of the club.

Ultimately, that's always going to create a friendly atmosphere, as their outlook on the club is as positive as anyone else's in the division. There are plenty of reasons for that, but the current ownership group allowing for celebrity co-owner involvement and investment will be the main catalyst.

Five players, five careers. Name them from the clubs they played for.

Each day there are five current or former EFL players to identify. For each one you’ll see their senior clubs in the order they played for them. Loan spells are left out.

You get four guesses per player. Every wrong guess unlocks a clue: first their position, then their nationality, then their initials.

Get it first time for 4 points, then 3, 2 and 1. The best possible score is 20. A new set of players arrives at midnight.

Outside of that, though, the recruitment drive, after the Swans entered a partnership with the Tony Bloom-led Jamestown Analytics, has created a squad for a fanbase to be proud of, which is led by the popular head coach, Vitor Matos.

After some years in the wilderness, there's a lot to like about the current Swansea setup. That creates a proud and friendly fanbase, according to AI.

Just below Swansea in that fan engagement index was Norwich, who do have quite an up-and-down relationship with the senior figures at the club, namely sporting director Ben Knapper.

But when things are going well for the Canaries, there isn't a nicer place for an away fan to go, as the fanbase is generally considered one of the friendliest in England by travelling supporters, according to AI's admission.

For the second successive year, it looks like Norwich have waited 12 games or so to find their groove, soaring up the Championship table with a handful of wins before the November international break.

Norwich fans are happy to talk about their club once more, and that can only strengthen their position on the podium of this specific list.

However, despite high fan engagement scores, AI believes that it's the Preston North End fanbase which is the friendliest in the Championship.

It states that there's "a degree of perspective" amongst the supporters due to their seasons of mid-table stability in the Championship, and that a lack of promotion demands can create a level-headed crowd.

Who do you think are the friendliest fans in the Championship? Let us know in the comments.

Unfortunately, things have seemingly started to go the other way, with PNE in relegation danger under Paul Heckingbottom a couple of years ago, and then this season, too, as the club underwent the worst 14-game start any Championship side has ever had.

But overall, Preston is highly regarded as a nice place to visit, and one of the cheaper places, too, with away tickets priced at £30 for that massive Kop end behind one of the goals. Plenty of away fans come up, and if not for the, quote, unquote, friendliest fanbase in the division, they wouldn't feel inclined to come back.

Jack Grealish's Everton travails could signal a death knell to the 'Gazza generation'

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Deep down, Jack Grealish will know that his fate extends beyond Everton's season.

English football’s first £100m player has won multiple Premier League titles and a Champions League yet still faces a fight to stave off the death knell to an increasingly lost generation as much as his own elite-level career.

Grealish again finds himself out in the cold after a ‘breach of club discipline’ in the run-up to Everton’s Carabao Cup win over Newcastle and was subsequently deemed unfit for the league rematch at St James’ Park on Monday night. On both occasions, David Moyes’s side were hardly poorer without the winger’s presence.

The Toffees are yet to decide on a definitive course of action over Grealish’s reported unauthorised absence from training, but patience is now in increasingly short supply from the club and its supporters alike. At a time when Moyes is dealing with crippling squad dearth - just 18 senior players were left after a disastrous summer window - the England international's latest misdemeanour threw him another needless curveball.

There was always a risk that Grealish's propensity to hit the headlines for the wrong reasons would again rear its head on Merseyside, where he claimed that feeling ‘loved’ in his initial loan spell was a driving factor in embarking on a second. Such was a fondness for his current surroundings that he undertook the bulk of his rehabilitation from a season-ending foot injury sustained in January at their Finch Farm training ground instead of returning to parent club Manchester City.

But even then, indiscretion was still lurking round the corner. When Everton slipped to a late April defeat at relegation candidates West Ham, Grealish was being snapped slumped against the arm of a chair in a Manchester rooftop bar surrounded by bottles and glasses. Now 31, he is in danger of completing an unholy trinity that was once touted for greatness as successors to Paul Gascoigne’s throne but allowed their careers to crash and burn.

Just 16 months separate the respective birth dates of the Birmingham-born talent, Dele Alli and Raheem Sterling. All three were alive when English football’s flawed genius enjoyed one last hurrah at Euro 96 but none will have any memories of it. That has still done little to stop their affection for ‘Gazzamania’ running deep.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

Upon his return to Everton this summer, Grealish revealed he was partly inspired to take the No.18 shirt worn by Gascoigne during a forgettable and fleeting spell in the blue half of Merseyside while Sterling also cited the man that personified the 1990 World Cup as his all-time favourite Three Lions player. Even Dele, who himself previously sought redemption at Everton, made an embarrassing attempt at recreating the infamous ‘dentist’s chair’ celebration during a training session at Euro 2016.

They also appeared destined to follow in his footsteps by earning moves to high-profile leagues overseas with Real Madrid heavily touted as a future destination for each player. Beyond personal homages, Gascoigne has become intertwined with the trio as a quickfire buzzword for their respective rises to prominence. Individual billings as the ‘new Gazza’ also proved to be a self-fulfilling prophecy for a penchant for self-destruction mirroring the downfall of the former Rangers and Lazio playmaker. Dele is now 14 months into unofficial retirement and resorting to calling up former teammates live on Twitch streams in a desperate ploy to stay in the public consciousness. An awkward attempted catch-up with Harry Kane, just weeks before his old Tottenham sparring partner claimed the Ballon d’Or, lasted barely 60 seconds.

The depth of Dele's continental ambitions was short-lived spells with Besiktas and Como, making a solitary appearance for the latter before being released barely midway through an 18-month contract. Free agent Sterling fared far worse with a three-month spell at Feyenoord which was terminated last season. Grealish's former City teammate and fellow Euro 2020 standout faces the real prospect of ending this month behind bars as well as without a club after pleading guilty to dangerous driving while inhaling nitrous oxide and crashing his Lamborghini on the A327 in May.

Without sufficient intervention on his own part, Grealish may soon follow his former contemporaries in falling by the footballing wayside. More than most, he has been the poster boy for Gascoigne's heirs, as the man himself confirmed to FourFourTwo last year by singling out the former Aston Villa graduate as his closest embodiment in the current crop of players. His exploits in City’s 2023 treble-winning campaign hinted that England’s first Ballon d’Or winner since Michael Owen was closer than Kane’s recent triumph but never came to pass. What happens next will be era-defining.

Grealish is still capable of technical excellency, as demonstrated in a truncated first loan spell with Everton, if he can stave off personal indiscipline issues. He would not be the first homegrown talent to do so. Ross Barkley, another ex-teammate born in the mid-90s, was also festooned with the Gascoigne moniker yet managed to overcome his own off-field issues to earn a second chance at Villa. Persistent injuries prevented the playmaker from scaling even a fraction of the heights he was originally tipped to reach but used stints at Nice and Luton Town to rebuild his reputation and earn a bit-part role with Unai Emery’s side.

By personal admission, Grealish has been his own worst enemy when it comes to a sense of timing off the pitch which is at odds with an often impeccable nature on it. Unless he can conquer those social demons which ultimately brought down his idol as well as those in the same talent pool, the epitaph of the 'Gazza generation' will be one of frustratingly unfulfilled potential.

The Caixa's first rebrand in almost 30 years builds on the existing foundations.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Banks don't always have the most exciting branding, but Brazil's Caixa Econômica Federal, a huge state-owned financial institution with 165 years of history, has a good go. The bank's rebranded for the first time in almost three decades, and the new identity ticks all the boxes.

The new identity is a fine example of a redesign done right. It feels more modern, more human and approachable, and it remains instantly recognisable, maintaining a clever hidden symbolism.

See our guides to the best laptops for graphic design and the best monitors for graphics artists if you need new tools for your own work.

The Caixa rebrand was the work of the Brazilian agency Tátil. It looked to the bank's history and its existing identity, kept the elements that people most recognised, and turned them up.

The previous logo design had lasted 29 years, so it was doing something right, but it was starting to look drab and dated with the old corporate feel of those muted colours and slightly italicised all-caps letters.

The rebrand turns the colours up with more modern, vibrant hues. Tátil suggests the blue represents enduring solidity while the orange reflects the diversity of Brazil.

The wordmark gets a cleaner lowercase design, and the core X motif, which features in the wordmark and also stands alone, gets a significant refresh.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

The letter now more clearly resembles a box when applied in repeating patterns, reflecting the bank's name, which means box (as in savings box) in Portuguese. It also continues to resemble the roof of a house, reflecting the bank's role as Brazil's biggest mortgage lender.

You can see the evolution of the institution's branding in the video below.

"While every app was busy calling itself a bank, we went against the grain and found what only Caixa has, a bank that brings together the social and the commercial, today's needs and the drive to grow," Tátil says of the rebrand.

NEAR AI Discloses SGLang Security Incident and Revokes Exposed API Keys

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: NEAR AI has published a security incident report involving an SGLang vulnerability in legacy deployments. The company says it revoked exposed credentials and addressed the issue, while acknowledging that some requests could have been observed. Its findings distinguish confirmed exposure from data it has no evidence was accessed.

A security disclosure from NEAR AI highlights a familiar danger in the expanding AI-agent industry: sophisticated applications can inherit serious problems from the infrastructure they run on.

In a November 9 incident report, the organization described an SGLang remote-code-execution vulnerability affecting legacy deployment infrastructure. The investigation identified roughly 100 user details and 18 agents in the affected context, with two Cloud API keys exposed and subsequently revoked.

The company said there was no evidence that stored Private Chat content had been compromised. It also cautioned that some requests made on November 8 could potentially have been observed. Those are not contradictory statements: investigators may find no evidence of one category of access while being unable to rule out another.

AI applications are assembled from multiple components: model servers, request routers, authentication systems, logging and the user-facing product. SGLang is part of the infrastructure used to serve language models. A flaw at that level can create opportunities for attackers before a user ever interacts with an onchain wallet or signs a transaction.

Remote-code-execution issues can be particularly serious because they may allow an attacker to run unauthorized instructions in the affected environment. The scope of what follows depends on the privileges, network access and data available to that environment.

For readers, this means the incident should not be conflated with a vulnerability in the NEAR blockchain consensus rules or a general compromise of all NEAR wallets. The report concerns particular AI-serving infrastructure and its potential exposures.

According to the published account, the team investigated affected deployments, revoked the two exposed API keys, remediated the vulnerability and notified impacted parties. Those are necessary containment steps, though the effectiveness of remediation depends on whether other pathways remain open and whether credentials are rotated wherever they were used.

The report’s numerical counts also need careful interpretation. Approximately 100 user details and 18 agents do not mean 100 users lost funds or that every associated request was read. Exposure, confirmed access and financial loss are separate findings that should not be collapsed into one sensational claim.

Crypto firms are integrating AI into development, support, trading and operational workflows. An AI tool may eventually handle private business information even when it cannot directly sign onchain transactions. That raises the value of conventional safeguards: isolating services, limiting credentials, monitoring access and maintaining incident-response plans.

The broader institutional push toward blockchain infrastructure, including the NYSE’s tokenized-securities plans, depends on more than smart-contract security. Offchain services must also be robust. A well-audited ledger cannot compensate for a compromised application server.

Likewise, rising institutional attention to crypto assets, which NewsBTC tracks through developments such as Ether ETF flows, makes operational controls increasingly consequential. These links are contextual rather than a claim that those products were involved in this incident.

Affected users should rely on NEAR AI’s direct notifications and published guidance. Where they have used credentials in related services, reviewing access permissions and rotating keys is prudent. Users should not assume that ordinary NEAR wallet keys have been exposed simply because the company reported an infrastructure flaw.

The most important follow-up is a clear technical postmortem explaining affected versions, root cause and validation of the fixes. For companies building AI agents around finance, the episode is a reminder that infrastructure security and candid disclosure are both part of earning trust.

Written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

Martin Zubimendi responds to Chelsea transfer speculation and reveals Mikel Arteta warning

📅 2026-11-11  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Martin Zubimend has revealed he was warned he would face a fight to maintain his status as a regular starter at Arsenal this season, but insists he hasn’t given up hope of winning back his place.

The Spain international joined the Gunners 18 months ago following a £60million move from Real Sociedad.

Zubimendi had come close to signing for Liverpool a year earlier, but was convinced to snub the Reds’ interest and delay a move to England, with Mikel Arteta outlining his determination to make him a key component of his midfield rebuild.

The 27-year-old’s decision to move to the north London club initially looked an inspired one as he played almost every game over the course of the first two thirds of a campaign that ultimately ended in glory.

Fatigue, however, eventually caught up with Zubimendi and his place was effectively taken by teenager Myles Lewis-Skelly during the run-in and he has been reduced to a bit-part role ever since.

Read exclusive analysis of the biggest stories in football with Metro's weekly football newsletter In The Mixer. Sign up now for free.

Arsenal’s decision to sanction the signing of Bruno Guimaraes has only served to further heighten the intense competition for places and, as a result, Zubimendi is yet to start a Premier League game this season.

That lack of game time has sparked intense speculation that a January move could be on the cards for the World Cup winner.

Chelsea, who are in the market for a midfielder and whose manager, Xabi Alonso, attempted to sign Zubimendi during his brief stint in charge of Real Madrid, are said to be considering renewing interest having made tentative enquiries during the summer.

Atletico Madrid, meanwhile, have been positioned as the frontrunners, with Diego Simeone said to be dreaming of signing the Arsenal star with reports in Spain going as far as saying that a deal is close.

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

Responding to a whirlwind of rumours, Zubimendi told Marca: ‘To be honest, I’m sometimes surprised by the press too. I understand that the winter transfer window is approaching and rumors can surface, but I think we need to be a bit more careful with what we publish.

‘Ultimately, everyone has a team they play for, and we need to respect that. I’ve also read that I might be close, but not at all.

‘Often a news story comes out and it seems that if it doesn’t come true, it’s quickly forgotten and nothing happens. I’m learning how this world works and getting used to it, but sometimes I could avoid a lot of the messages I receive because of this kind of news.’

Asked whether his current predicament has come as a surprise, Zubimendi revealed Arteta had been totally transparent at the season’s outset and outlined the role he envisaged for a player he fought so hard to sign.

He said:  ‘I spoke with Mikel at the beginning of the season. As he does with all the players, he explained the plan to me.

‘He warned me that there were new signings and that there would be playing time for everyone. And here we are, fighting for it.

‘I understand because, for me, we’re among the five best teams in Europe. There are some fantastic players. New signings have arrived in my position, and there were already some very good players. I’m also part of that competition that makes it so difficult to get playing time.’

He added:  ‘I’m calm and happy with how I’m handling this situation. It’s something I’ve never experienced before, and I think you learn from these situations too.

‘Besides, it’s in moments like these that you need the people around you the most, and they’re there for you.

‘I’m patient. When you’ve been playing a lot of minutes and your role changes to not playing as much, I’m able to see the positive side. I think everything passes, that just like last year the opposite happened to me and I played practically everything, this can change very quickly.’

Telecom Review Group accelera l'implementazione di una connettività a 10 Giga nel GCC con l'U6GHz

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

DUBAI, Emirati Arabi Uniti, 11 novembre 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Alla 20a edizione del Telecom Review Leaders' Summit, tenutosi il 20 e il 21 ottobre 2026 presso il Ritz-Carlton, Dubai Jumeirah Beach Residence, i leader del settore hanno esaminato come lo spettro della banda superiore a 6 GHz (U6GHz) e la tecnologia 5G-Advanced potrebbero accelerare il percorso del GCC verso la connettivitÃ  a 10 Gbps.

Moderato da Issam Eid, direttore marketing del gruppo e partner del Telecom Review Group, il panel ha riunito il Dr. Bilel Jamoussi, vice-direttore del Telecommunication Standardization Bureau dell'UIT e capo dipartimento dei gruppi di studio e delle politiche (SPD); Jawad Abbassi, responsabile per la regione MENA della GSMA; l'Ing. Saif Bin Ghalita, direttore esecutivo per lo sviluppo tecnologico della TDRA; il Dr. Mahmoud Sherif, responsabile per l'innovazione e lo sviluppo commerciale di Technico, du; il Dr. Ayman Elnashar, vice-presidente per la strategia tecnologica, l'architettura e l'innovazione di etisalat; Steven Liu, vice-presidente per il marketing e le vendite delle soluzioni ICT di Huawei Medio Oriente e Asia centrale; e Omar Samman, responsabile per la regione del Golfo di Ericsson.

La politica in materia di spettro costituisce la base

I progressi compiuti dagli Emirati Arabi Uniti nell'implementazione commerciale dell'U6GHz riflettono l'importanza di una politica lungimirante in materia di spettro e di un coordinamento normativo. L'Ing. Bin Ghalita ha delineato la tabella di marcia del Paese in materia di spettro, tra cui l'assegnazione di 500 MHz nella banda di 6 GHz per il Wi-Fi indoor nel 2020 e la successiva designazione di 700 MHz, che coprono l'intervallo di 6,425-7,125 GHz, per le telecomunicazioni mobili internazionali (IMT) nel piano nazionale delle frequenze del 2024.

Queste misure hanno contribuito ad aprire la via alle implementazioni commerciali dell'U6GHz da parte di etisalat e du. "Per il lancio commerciale dell'U6GHz, vorrei ringraziare in modo particolare Huawei, con cui gli Emirati Arabi Uniti mantengono una relazione da piÃ¹ di 25 anni. Huawei ha sempre mantenuto il suo impegno", ha sottolineato Bin Ghalita. "Questo tipo di collaborazione Ã¨ ciÃ² che trasforma le nostre missioni in progressi tangibili".

Le autoritÃ  di regolamentazione devono inoltre affrontare la compatibilitÃ  dei dispositivi e il coordinamento delle interferenze.

"Il lavoro delle autoritÃ  di regolamentazione non si esaurisce con lo spettro assegnato; continua fino a quando l'investimento e il partenariato non si traducono in un'esperienza migliore per la societÃ ", ha osservato l'Ing. Bin Ghalita.

La GSMA ha sottolineato il crescente slancio internazionale a favore dell'U6GHz, con Paesi che rappresentano oltre l'80% della popolazione mondiale che ora supportano l'uso mobile della banda e piÃ¹ di 60 operatori dell'ecosistema mobile che supportano l'implementazione su larga scala. Per far progredire il percorso dell'industria verso la 6G, Abbassi ha sottolineato l'importanza di incoraggiare l'adozione dello spettro, al contempo riconoscendo e dimostrando il piÃ¹ ampio valore economico della distribuzione dell'U6GHz con licenza.

"L'approccio con licenza genera vantaggi economici 7 volte superiori rispetto al mantenimento nella banda senza licenza", ha osservato Abbassi.

Il Dr. Jamoussi ha avvertito che approcci nazionali divergenti potrebbero frammentare gli ecosistemi dei dispositivi e delle reti, aumentando i costi. "L'armonizzazione internazionale sarÃ  essenziale per rendere la connettivitÃ  avanzata piÃ¹ conveniente e interoperabile", ha affermato.

Trasformare il potenziale di 10 Gbps in prestazioni reali

La larghezza di banda ultra-larga di U6GHz sta consentendo significativi progressi nelle velocitÃ  massime di trasmissione dei dati, con du che riporta una velocitÃ  massima di trasmissione dei dati di 10 Gbps in una dimostrazione 5G-Advanced che combina lo spettro U6GHz e Sub-3 GHz. Il Dr. Sherif ha posizionato il risultato come una pietra miliare per il piÃ¹ ampio ecosistema delle telecomunicazioni degli Emirati Arabi Uniti.

"Questo risultato Ã¨ particolarmente degno di nota dato il ruolo di 5G-Advanced nel fare da ponte tra 5G e 6G. Ci sono cosÃ¬ tanti aspetti fondamentali che possiamo valutare nell'ambito della commercializzazione della 6G", ha sottolineato il Dr. Sherif.

Steven Liu ha identificato l'accesso wireless fisso (FWA), la banda larga mobile e i servizi basati sull'IA come potenziali aree di applicazione, evidenziando come la larghezza di banda ultra-larga dell'U6GHz supporti la banda larga domestica ad alta velocitÃ  e le applicazioni mobili esigenti, anche se le prestazioni effettive dipenderanno dalla configurazione della rete e dalle condizioni di distribuzione. Steven Liu ha sottolineato che l'industria puÃ² "portare un nuovo livello di servizio innovativo e un'esperienza superiore per l'utente finale sul mercato attraverso l'U6GHz".

Il Dr. Elnashar ha evidenziato le offerte aziendali emergenti e le applicazioni di consumo basate sull'IA. "Ã un percorso che abbiamo iniziato due o tre anni fa con il primo prototipo di U6GHz negli Emirati Arabi Uniti", ha osservato. "Dopo la prova in cui abbiamo dimostrato 10 Gbps in downlink e uplink, credo che stiamo colmando il divario tra wireless e fibra per la prima volta".

Omar Samman ha evidenziato le crescenti richieste di uplink e bassa latenza da parte delle applicazioni IA. "La crescita di uplink non Ã¨ piÃ¹ una previsione; sta giÃ  accadendo", ha affermato, sottolineando il ruolo del software di rete di accesso radio abilitato all'IA e l'aggiornamento della pianificazione dell'area di servizio.

In seguito al panel, Allen Tang, presidente di Wireless Network & Cloud Core Network Marketing & Solution Sales di Huawei, e Toni Eid, fondatore di Telecom Review Group e CEO di Trace Media International, si sono uniti ai relatori per segnare ufficialmente il primo lancio commerciale U6GHz al mondo durante una cerimonia esclusiva, evidenziando i progressi degli Emirati Arabi Uniti nel trasferire la tecnologia dalla pianificazione dello spettro alla distribuzione commerciale.

La pietra miliare ha sottolineato l'ambizione della regione di accelerare il passaggio a una societÃ  a 10 Gbps. Per realizzare tale visione, tuttavia, saranno necessarie infrastrutture convergenti, norme armonizzate, dispositivi pronti all'uso e investimenti sostenibili. Questi fattori determineranno l'efficacia con cui il CCG traduce il potenziale dell'U6GHz in connettivitÃ  reale e sostiene la transizione verso la 6G.

Gram Is Only Bright Spot in Crypto Bloodbath, Durov Says

📅 2026-11-11  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Telegram founder Pavel Durov recently drew attention to the remarkable performance of The Open Network (TON) cryptocurrency Gram (GRAM) during the massive sell-off across the crypto space.

In his latest post on the social media platform X, Durov shared that Gram was bucking the bearish market and had risen by 8% while other major cryptocurrencies were still struggling.

"Crypto's a sea of red. Only Gram is up +8%," the billionaire said.

The Telegram founder explained that Gram is doing better thanks to its integration with Telegram. He argues that Gram has a serious advantage over other cryptocurrencies due to its connection to an existing social media ecosystem.

The billionaire entrepreneur was extremely bullish on the future of Gram, claiming that it deserves to be among the top three largest digital assets by market capitalization.

The Telegram founder said Gram was "still ridiculously undervalued," adding that he believes Gram will eventually get into CoinMarketCap's top three.

However, Durov's claim that only Gram is rising is not entirely correct.

Gram is currently trading around the $1.15 price level, according to CoinGecko data. Over the last 24 hours, Gram is up by 4.5%. Its market capitalization is now around $3.28 billion. It ranks 29th.

Of course, Gram is one of the best performers among major cryptocurrencies, but there are altcoins that have logged even stronger gains. For example, Bitway (BTW) is up by 18.8%. Lighter (LIT) is up by 10.8%. Internet Computer (ICP) is up by 8.2% over the last 24 hours.

There have been some major developments in the Telegram crypto ecosystem.

For example, in June, a big change happened when the native cryptocurrency of TON officially changed its name from Toncoin (TON) to Gram (GRAM). Thus, the project restored the name that was first proposed for Telegram's blockchain project. A total of 81.22% of votes were in favor of the name change. Users don't need to swap their tokens.

Telegram is also working to increase adoption of its native self-custodial Gram Wallet to let users send crypto directly inside the messenger.

On Nov. 9, Durov said that more than 700K self-custodial wallets had been created in Telegram over 48 hours.

The Telegram founder said that it's possible to get Gram using wallet auto-activation without complex activation procedures.

There is also significant institutional interest in the ecosystem. For example, Nasdaq-listed TON Strategy said that it held about 235.1 million GRAM tokens at the end of Q3. The company reported $13.1 million in quarterly revenue on Nov. 10.

Fujino scores twice as Manchester City win 4-1 at Inter in the Champions League

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

To help deliver timely coverage of women's football, we may use AI tools to support content creation and editing. While we strive for accuracy and editorial quality, readers should independently verify important information.

Manchester City beat Inter Milano 4-1 at the Felice Chinetti Stadium in Varese on Tuesday 10 November, in the fourth round of the Women’s Champions League league stage. Aoba Fujino scored twice before the interval, and Kerstin Casparij and Grace Clinton added second-half goals.

Inter, set up in a 5-4-1 by Gianpiero Piovani, struck first. In the 24th minute Ivana Andrés provided the assist and Elisa Bartoli finished to put the Italian side ahead.

The lead lasted nine minutes. Fujino levelled in the 33rd minute from a Laura Blindkilde Brown pass, and in the 44th minute she scored again, this time without an assist, so Andrée Alexander Jeglertz’s side went in 2-1 up at half-time.

City added two more goals after the break. Casparij made it 3-1 in the 56th minute, set up by Samantha Coffey. Clinton then scored the fourth in the 70th minute, with Lauren Hemp providing the assist.

Between those goals, Risa Shimizu was booked for a foul in the 67th minute. That was the only card shown by referee Marta Huerta de Aza in a game with six fouls on each side.

The numbers reflect the scoreline. City had 60% of the ball and completed 556 of their 603 passes, while Inter completed 335 of 393. The visitors had 21 shots to 16 and put 11 on target, compared with 6 for Inter. Their expected goals figure of 3.45 was almost three times Inter’s 1.19.

The margin could have been wider. Inter goalkeeper Rúnarsdóttir made 7 saves. At the other end Yamashita made 5 saves, and Inter won 4 corners to City’s 2.

City have now won three matches in a row. Before this win they beat West Ham 5-0 and London City Lionesses 2-1. They are unbeaten in six games in all competitions, a run that includes 1-1 draws with Paris Saint Germain and Manchester United.

For Inter, the result ended a two-match winning run. They had beaten both AC Milan and Lazio 3-0 in their previous two outings. It is also their second heavy defeat in six games, after a 6-0 loss to Lyon.

There is little time to recover for either side. City return to the WSL with a trip to Chelsea on Saturday 14 November. Inter host Juventus in Serie A Women the following day, Sunday 15 November.

Swarmer (SWMR) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Acquisitions and air defense plans reshape Swarmer's strategy toward hardware.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Swarmer, Inc (SWMR -2.23%) reported a strategic transition toward becoming an end-to-end provider of autonomous defense hardware and software for allied nations. Management announced the pending acquisition of Ratel Robotics, a major manufacturer of unmanned ground vehicles in Ukraine, and the planned acquisition of intellectual property from Vataga Drone. The company also signed a memorandum of understanding to manufacture complete air defense systems for Vectus Air Defense Systems. These developments are intended to capture the entire value chain of autonomous systems, moving beyond the company's original software-only licensing model. Management indicated that these hardware and software capabilities will be unified into integrated kits starting in Q1 2027.

Operator: Good afternoon. Welcome to the Swarmer, Inc. Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Conference Call. Joining us for today's presentation are the company's President and U.S. CEO, Alex Fink; and Chief Financial Officer, Brooks Ensign. [Operator Instructions] Before we begin, I want to remind everyone that today's call will include forward-looking statements within the meaning of the federal securities laws. These statements include, among others, statements regarding Swarmer's strategy, market opportunity, customer engagement, product development, technology integrations, expansion into new markets, future revenue opportunities, expected customer mix, potential deployments and the anticipated benefits of the company's relationships, memorandum of understanding, partnerships and commercial initiatives.

Forward-looking statements are based on current expectations and assumptions and are subject to risks and uncertainties that could cause actual results to differ materially. Additional information about factors that could cause actual results to differ is included in the company's earnings release issued today and in the company's filings with the Securities and Exchange Commission, including the risk factors described in those filings. The company undertakes no obligation to update the forward-looking statements, except as required by law. Finally, I'd like to remind everyone that this conference call is being webcasted, and a recording will be made available for replay on the company's Investor Relations website.

In addition to the webcast, the company has posted a press release that accompanies these results, which can also be found on the Investor Relations website. I will now turn the call over to Swarmer's President and U.S. CEO, Alex Fink, for his comments. Sir, please proceed.

Alexander Fink: Thank you, operator, and thank you, everyone, for joining us. The third quarter was pivotal for Swarmer. Over the past year, we have spoken frequently about our vision to build a platform for combat-tested technologies from Ukraine to spread to all allied nations. We have taken meaningful steps towards realizing this vision. Most notably, we announced our pending acquisition of Ratel Robotics, one of the largest manufacturers of unmanned ground vehicles in Ukraine. Ratel accounted for 37% of all purchases of unmanned ground vehicles by Ukraine's government in the previous procurement cycle in spring 2026.

We also announced the pending acquisition of intellectual property and R&D personnel from Vataga Drone, which adds multiple models of combat-tested quadcopters, interceptors and initiation boards to our hardware portfolio. Finally, we signed a memorandum of understanding to manufacture and deliver complete air defense systems for Vectus Air Defense. Throughout all this, we have strengthened our cash position and finished the quarter with more than $37 million of cash on our balance sheet compared to approximately $25 million in Q2. These developments are a major expansion. Swarmer is no longer a software provider who relies on drone manufacturers to gain access to the battlefield.

It is becoming a direct seller of end-to-end solutions with a growing portfolio of ground vehicles, quadcopters, interceptors and air defense systems that can be deployed in Ukraine and in all other allied nations. We are making the strategic, proactive investment to own the entire value chain end-to-end. We know there is enormous demand for autonomous drones and autonomous interceptors. We know the military units that want it, and we know they are willing to pay for it. And with the pending Vataga deal, we should soon have the intellectual property and the hardware team to fulfill this demand with a complete kit where autonomy is built in and not bolted on.

We believe this complete end-to-end kit can be codified in Q1 and help make autonomy more widely adopted on the Ukrainian battlefield. This does not mean we are stepping away from software sales or from vendor-agnostic solutions that can be used by any drone maker in the market, but it means we are no longer relying on the strategy exclusively. I expect us to focus more heavily on B2G sales, selling directly to the Ukrainian government and to other governments around the world, with B2B software sales becoming one out of many revenue streams and not the primary one. With this in mind, I'd like to cover Ratel Robotics in more detail.

The definitive agreement was signed on September 9, and our shareholders approved the related share issuance on November 2. We expect the deal to close in November 2026 as there are no governmental approvals required. We have Ratel's audited financials through Q2 of this year and their unaudited financials for Q3 as well. Ratel's year-to-date revenue for the first 3 quarters of 2026 was $42.1 million, with $32.5 million in the third quarter. Moreover, $14.9 million were booked in September alone. While these numbers are unaudited, I believe they reflect the growth that the company is undergoing as it is increasing manufacturing capacity to fulfill its existing $86 million of previously signed government contracts.

With this, I'm comfortable providing revenue guidance for Q4 this year, estimating that Ratel's revenue for Q4 will likely be between $35 million and $40 million with operating margins of between 13% and 17%. Our definitive agreement with Ratel makes the majority of their consideration contingent in the form of earn-outs on Ratel's continued ability to grow the revenue and operating income. The revenue targets set in the agreement are $77 million in 2026, $130 million in 2027 and $187 million in 2028, with operating income targets of $8.47 million, $14.3 million and $20.57 million, respectively.

There is an additional bonus payment due if Ratel reaches a revenue target of $249 million in 2028, along with an operating income target of $27.39 million. Ratel's willingness to commit to these targets and make its consideration contingent on hitting them shows that they are confident in their ability to achieve these targets. They are manufacturing at full capacity, and they are increasing their manufacturing capacity each quarter as we have seen from the Q3 numbers. We have seen evidence of this team's ability to perform, and we believe in Taras Ostapchuk and his team.

While we understand the uncertainty of geopolitics and of Ukraine's future budgets, we believe that this team will do everything within their control to deliver on their commitments. We are further encouraged by the announcement of cooperation between Ratel and General Dynamics as well as discussions with other manufacturers in Germany, Denmark, Netherlands and other countries that have joined the Build With Ukraine program, which could provide additional manufacturing capacity and additional sales outside of Ukraine. Our own Q3 results do not reflect these numbers, but we have a high degree of confidence that our results from Q4 onwards will reflect them going forward. In parallel, we continue to make progress in air defense.

Earlier this year, we became a founding partner and 20% equity holder in Vectus Air Defense Systems. The rest is held by an entity managed by Erik Prince, the founder of Blackwater and our Non-Executive Chairman. During the quarter, we announced a memorandum of understanding under which Swarmer intends to manufacture and sell complete air defense systems for Vectus. Importantly, Vectus has confirmed its intent to purchase at least 40 systems during the first year following the start of production and 80 or more systems during the second year, subject to definitive agreements and customary approvals. The MSRP of each system is estimated to be between $5 million and $6 million.

So we believe it is fair to expect that air defense will become an important pillar of our future earnings profile. These are the 3 pillars of our current market position: ground vehicle, air defense and autonomous drone kits that combine both hardware and software, all built from combat-tested real technologies that have already had an impact on Ukraine's battlefield. With that, I will turn it over to Brooks to walk over the financials in more detail.

Brooks Ensign: Thank you, Alex. Before I review the quarter, I'd like to remind everyone that additional details regarding our financial results can be found in today's earnings release and our Form 10-Q to be filed this week with the SEC. For the third quarter ended September 30, 2026, revenue was approximately $26,000 compared with approximately $36,000 in the prior year period. Revenue recognized during the period was primarily associated with support services provided under existing customer agreements. We reported a gross loss of approximately $47,000 in the third quarter of 2026 compared with a gross loss of approximately $10,000 in the prior year third quarter. Cost of revenue primarily reflected a fixed allocation of cloud storage costs.

Operating expenses were approximately $9.4 million compared with approximately $0.8 million in the prior year period. The increase reflects investments we are making to build the foundation for future growth, including expanding our engineering and product development capabilities, scaling our operational infrastructure, supporting our public company requirements and pursuing strategic initiatives such as the pending acquisition of Ratel Robotics. Operating expenses during the quarter also included approximately $1.7 million of noncash stock-based compensation expense. Net loss for the quarter was approximately $9.3 million compared with a net loss of approximately $3.4 million in the prior year period. Turning to the balance sheet.

We ended the quarter with approximately $37.3 million in cash and cash equivalents compared with $25.3 million at June 30, 2026. The increase in cash was primarily driven by capital raised during the quarter from our equity line of credit. We remain committed to managing our capital responsibly while investing in opportunities that we believe can generate long-term shareholder value. And with that, I'll turn the call over to the operator for questions. Operator?

Operator: [Operator Instructions] Now our first question will come from Alex Fuhrman with Lucid Capital Markets.

Alex Fuhrman: Alex, I was hoping you could help me understand your current mix of business and how the Ratel acquisition is going to change that. From what I read in the media, it seems like there's obviously some concern that Democratic control of Congress could potentially put some pressure on defense budgets broadly, but it certainly seems like Ukraine specifically could be a bright spot. There was a Reuters article recently, I think, that quoted Democratic aides in both chambers saying that funding for Ukraine could be a big priority for the new Congress. If I'm not mistaken, I believe all of your revenues to date have been in Ukraine.

As you think about Ratel and obviously, they have a road map to several hundred million in sales here over the next couple of years, how much of that projection is based on the war in Ukraine versus sales to the U.S. or other countries in Europe? Just can you help us understand that mix of business, please?

Alexander Fink: Yes. Thank you so much for this question. Right now, to date, all of our revenue has been in Ukraine, all of Ratel's revenue and the current guidance that was provided is in Ukraine. And the same is true of Vataga. So indeed, I think that we are somewhat different from other defense companies in the U.S. in that most of them rely on procurement in the U.S. and rely on defense budgets in the U.S., whereas we to date have relied mostly on Ukraine and as we think about expanding to air defense into other markets, those are worldwide markets that are also not U.S.-centric. So I hope that helps people understand and view this more broadly.

I think, obviously, when we're looking at General Dynamics or other partnerships in the future, those may sell worldwide as well and to the U.S. as well. But everything within the short to intermediate term that has been discussed so far has been in Ukraine.

Alex Fuhrman: Okay. That's really helpful, thank you for that clarification, Alex. And then just as you think about Ratel, I think you've mentioned previously even ahead of the transaction closing, it sounds like Ratel has been working on prototypes of UGVs that would, kind of, expand beyond their existing use cases and more getting into launching UAVs from them. Can you give us an update on how much progress has been made there? Have there been any successful launches of UAVs from UGVs yet?

Alexander Fink: So they have had successful prototype launches, which you can find in their social media. They've also had successful mounting of machine guns and other military hardware on UGVs and many other use cases like demining. Again, all of this, I can just refer people to Ratel social media. They can speak for themselves. So they have clearly been quite prolific when it comes to prototyping and experimentation. Obviously, if the deal closes as we anticipate, we will be working together on scaling and productizing this in the best way.

Operator: At this time, this concludes our question-and-answer session. If you have any additional questions, you may contact Swarmer's Investor Relations team at [email protected]. I'd now like to turn the call back over to Mr. Fink for his closing remarks.

Alexander Fink: Thanks again, everyone, for joining us today. As a reminder, you can find out more about our company, receive additional updates and learn about upcoming events from the Investor Relations section of our website. We look forward to updating you on the exciting progress we are making in the defense technology market. Finally, I'd like to thank our employees, partners and shareholders for their continued support. Operator?

JR assembles 2,500 portraits into a collective image of thailand’s creative scene

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

photographed across eleven professional sectors, the installation brings together artists, designers, architects, craftspeople, and the many people working behind the scenes of thailand’s cultural landscape.

JR’s participatory art project Inside Out comes to Cloud 11 with Thailand Creative Archive, a large-scale photographic installation featuring more than 2,500 black-and-white portraits of people working across the country’s creative industries. Opening on November 11th, 2026, the largest presentation of the Inside Out Project in Southeast Asia brings together artists, designers, architects, performers, craftspeople, and behind-the-scenes professionals in a collective portrait that places the people shaping the cultural landscape of the country at the center of public space.

Founded by JR, the Inside Out Project uses large-scale black-and-white portraits to give individuals and communities a platform to express their identities, beliefs, and causes. Since its launch, the initiative has reached more than 150 countries, inviting people to become active participants in shaping the visual identity of their communities.

The approach has informed several of JR’s best-known projects, including Portrait of a Generation, which brought the faces of young people from the Paris suburbs into public view, and La Caverne du Pont Neuf, which transformed Paris’s historic bridge into a cave-like installation.

For Thailand Creative Archive, the focus shifts to the people behind the creative economy of the country. Between May and September 2026, an Inside Out Photobooth truck traveled across the country, photographing more than 2,500 participants from eleven professional groups. These include music, art, film, software, gaming and animation, advertising, design, architecture, fashion, culinary arts, crafts and handicrafts, and other creative professions.

People whose contributions to the creative sector take different forms and levels of visibility stand in front of the French artist‘s camera lens. Artists, performers, and established creatives appear alongside photographers, production crews, designers, and professionals whose work many times remains outside the public eye. Presented together, the photographs shift attention away from individual authorship and toward the wider network of people that makes cultural production possible.

The archive presents Thailand’s creative identity through the people who collectively sustain the industry. The portraits document a particular moment in the cultural landscape of the country, raising a question about how creative communities are represented.

At Cloud 11, the photographs occupy several parts of the development, including its main facade, the exhibition space on Level 2, and the retail area. ‘We believe the success of the creative sector is never the achievement of one person alone. It is built on the collective efforts of everyone involved, whether in the spotlight or behind the scenes,’ says Paul Sirisant, CEO of Cloud 11. He adds that the project reflects the diversity of Thailand’s creative industry and the importance of recognizing the contributions of its wider community.

How AI worsens online violence against Middle East women

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Women in the Middle East already have to deal with an above-average share of online harassment and violence. The proliferation of AI-enabled tools is making it worse.

It happens all the time, says Kholoud al-Ameri, an Iraqi journalist and the editor of a news website.

"Female journalists here are often getting messages, and mostly from men," she explains. "They somehow find you online and then they say they just want to talk or they want to add you on Instagram or Facebook," says al-Ameri, who also heads a network of female journalists working on climate change stories. "Sometimes it's hard because the men say they're our readers and we don't want to discourage them. But I usually tell the women just to block these guys."

Syrian activist Yaman Amer tells of similar experiences as a result of writing about women's rights online — she says she gets hundreds of critical comments. "When I talk about women's rights online in a patriarchal society, that's just how things are," Amer, who is studying to be a lawyer in Damascus, told DW. "Not that I accept it. Out of every thousand offensive comments, there's usually one or two that make me feel like I have to report them because they're particularly vile. As in, they're extremely vulgar or threatening. And then it's more than just harassment. It's on a whole other level, humiliating and degrading."

Online harassment of women isn't unusual anywhere. Studies by a variety of organizations show that somewhere between 40% and 60% of all women worldwide have experienced it, while 65% have witnessed it.

There is no single standard for assessing the level of online harassment because surveys often use different types of questions or research methods. But in general, women around the Middle East appear to be dealing with a higher-than-global-average level of what is known as "technology facilitated gender-based violence," or TFGBV.

The United Nations Population Fund defines TFGBV as a harmful act "that is committed, assisted, aggravated or amplified ... by the use of information communication technologies," or other digital tools, "against a person on the basis of their gender."

According to research by UN Women, the UN body dedicated to gender equality, 68% of women in the Middle East have had to deal with it, while almost all — close to 100% — have seen it online.

In a 2025 study, UN Women found that 49% of women using the internet in the Middle East don't feel safe from online harassment. The number goes up to almost 70% if the women are more publicly visible activists, journalists or politicians.

And it is already clear that the increased availability of artificial intelligence-powered applications is making online harassment worse.

AI has the potential to supercharge this kind of abuse, the UN Women briefing points out, "both by facilitating the spread of targeted disinformation as well as the proliferation of image-based abuse and deepfake pornographic videos."

Of the women who had experienced online violence while working, around a quarter had already identified "abuse that was generated or amplified by AI tools," the briefing said.

The potential for using the latest technology in order to harass people online has always been there, says Siddharth Venkataramakrishnan, an analyst at the London-based Institute for Strategic Dialogue (ISD), which investigates extremism. "But it's just become easier to produce harmful content at scale and in a hyper-targeted way. And the production of non-consensual imagery is a real marker of the democratization of that technology, so it's moving away from primarily targeting celebrities to just regular people."

Venkataramakrishnan has been researching incidents that occurred at the beginning of 2026 when Grok, the AI-powered chatbot belonging to Elon Musk's company SpaceXAI, became able to alter photographs to depict a fully dressed subject as nearly naked. The scandal caused Grok's services to be restricted but, as Venkataramakrishnan pointed out, safeguards across different AI models tend to be inconsistent and users can still find work-arounds to get similar results.

In conservative cultures in the Middle East, the threat of this kind of half-naked picture of a woman being published has serious real-life implications, mostly because of what is and isn't acceptable for women around the region.

What makes the increase in AI-generated fakes even more problematic in the Middle East is that online abuse also has a higher chance of having offline effects.

"Norms related to honor, family reputation and male control can turn digital abuse into real-world consequences," a June briefing by the World Bank notes.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

It's also often not easy for people targeted with this kind of abuse to find legal protection or even somewhere to report it. While some Middle Eastern countries have cybercrime laws, most don't specifically address gender-based online violence nor have they been updated to grapple with AI-related abuse.

Last year's UN Women survey found that 36% of women said they were just told to ignore the online violence, while 23% were blamed for it themselves and 12% were subjected to physical punishment by their families because of it.

Other studies indicate that only about 7% of women who experienced technology-facilitated violence later report it to law enforcement. Various concerns stop them from doing so, including having to present sexualized images or answer personal questions in front of strangers — often also anathema to many women in the region.

The women who had experienced online harassment said they would often just block the perpetrators and around a quarter said they then became more careful about what they posted online. Twenty percent deleted their accounts or left social media altogether.

The rapid growth in AI-enabled harassment can also be more problematic in the Middle East because of the situations the women targeted find themselves living in.

Displacement, war, conflict and instability can all make women more vulnerable to online abuse and exploitation. Even the rapid development of AI-related technologies in a country without corresponding legal guardrails or digital literacy can increase the risk.

For example, observers point out that Saudi Arabia, a generally conservative country with strict rules for female behavior, is going through an accelerated program of digital transformation.

And in Syria, after the ousting of the dictatorial Assad regime, many Syrians "rushed into social media to speak freely," journalist Zaina Erhaim wrote for the Arab Reform Initiative in July this year.

Previously. digital polices had been closely policed by Syria's dictator and a political post could see the writer thrown in jail. But now Syrians "are navigating this newfound liberty without guardrails, oversight or protection," Erhaim argues, and female activists are paying a high price for it, especially where "gender intersects with sect."

In a policy paper published in May, the Syrian Women's Political Movement explained just how bad online abuse had become. In focus groups with 120 women from all around Syria, the majority of participants agreed that digital abuse had become a way of shutting them up. Younger activists reported personal pictures were being stolen from social media, then used against them.

"Smear campaigns targeting women's 'honor' and systemic defamation are actively deployed as punitive measures against any women who attempt to express a critical political opinion," the organization wrote, arguing that what was needed was a law that criminalized digital violence.

If you or someone you know have experienced any kind of gender-based violence, the website lila.help (https://lila.help) lists trustworthy helplines and NGOs that offer support in almost every country around the world.

SpaceX launches Starlink Mini 2 with a swappable battery and Wi-Fi 6

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Invitation-only US preorders cost $999 with a year of Roam Unlimited included, while backend code points to a $249 kit.

The Starlink Mini 2, announced November 10, 2026, with its removable battery pack attached.

The original Starlink Mini was designed to make satellite internet portable, but it still needed an external power source. SpaceX has addressed that limitation with the Starlink Mini 2, announced on Tuesday, which has a detachable battery that clips onto the back of the dish and provides up to 4.5 hours of use.

The 71Wh battery weighs 490g and can be attached or removed without tools. It has its own USB-C port and LED indicators showing the charge level, allowing users to charge a spare separately and swap batteries when needed.

The dish itself charges through a 60W USB-C connection, replacing the proprietary power cable used by the original Mini. A physical power button also replaces the previous plug-and-play setup.

SpaceX has upgraded other parts of the hardware too. The Mini 2 uses Wi-Fi 6 instead of Wi-Fi 5, increases coverage from 112 to around 167 square metres and is thinner, measuring 23.1mm compared with 38.5mm for the original. With the battery and kickstand attached, it weighs 1.6kg and retains its IP67 rating.

The pricing, however, is less straightforward. SpaceX opened preorders on Tuesday to selected customers in the US, offering the device for $999 with a year of Roam Unlimited service included. Shipping is expected to begin in mid-December.

There may be a cheaper option once the device becomes generally available. Backend code on Starlink’s website lists a $249 Mini 2 beta kit and a $50 spare battery, according to YouTuber DISHYtech, as Digital Trends reported.

SpaceX has not published standard retail prices, so the figures appear to refer to different packages. The $999 offer includes a year of service, while the $249 listing seems to cover the hardware.

Including a year of connectivity in the launch offer is a first for Starlink. The original Mini currently starts at $149 in the US, but its reliance on an external power source led to a market for third-party batteries that could be attached to the dish. SpaceX has now incorporated that feature into its own hardware.

A battery-powered dish is particularly suited to travellers, campers, van owners and sailors, who are among the customers targeted by Starlink’s Roam plans. It also gives the company a product to sell in markets where it is expanding, including Vietnam.

Starlink has become a major part of SpaceX’s connectivity business, which helped underpin the company’s IPO filing this year.

The Mini 2 gives customers another reason to choose the service, removing the need to carry a separate power source. For now, however, the $999 offer is available only to invited US customers.

Introducing Mini 2, the next-generation portable Starlink. Get up to 4.5 hours of connectivity on a single charge with the quick-swap battery, or plug in with USB-C.

With built-in Wi-Fi, all you need is a clear view of the sky to get online in 60 seconds.

Preorders are now open by invitation in the US. Be the first to know when it’s available → https://t.co/t2pSI1buaW

Is XRP the Best Cryptocurrency You Can Buy Right Now?

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

XRP's price may be lower than before, but its upside bids are starting to look improbable.

With its price down by 70% from its all-time high set in mid-2025, it's natural for investors to wonder if XRP (XRP -4.33%) is priced at a bargain, and perhaps even one so deep as to make it the best cryptocurrency that it's possible to buy. But price isn't the factor that determines whether an asset is a bargain; the fundamentals are the more important part.

On that front, Ripple, XRP's issuer, has spent a handful of years and considerable effort courting banks and other financial institutions around the globe. Ripple has also worked extensively on expanding the capabilities of XRP's blockchain, the XRP Ledger (XRPL). Has any of that work actually made XRP a more appealing coin to buy?

Financial institutions are evaluating blockchain technology to manage tokenized assets.

Tokenization is the process of recording the ownership data of an asset, such as a Treasury bill or a stock, on a crypto token that's held and exchanged on a blockchain. The XRP Ledger was home to $642.9 million in tradeable tokenized assets as of Nov. 9, making it the ninth-largest among blockchain networks. Ethereum, the destination for the biggest collection of those assets, hosted $15.1 billion, so the XRPL is very far from being a leader despite developing its own tech explicitly to make its tokenized asset features more appealing to banks.

What's more, just $22.5 million worth of tokenized assets changed hands on the network in the 30 days ending Nov. 9. And that volume was incredibly lumpy, too; in the last week of October, only $4,620 in tokenized assets were traded. That's a strong sign that there isn't really an active ecosystem of tokenized assets, as the overwhelming majority of the tradeable assets on the chain are sitting inert.

The data on another potential growth area for XRP, AI agents, largely tell the same story. The XRPL's AI Hub recorded a cumulative total of $58,683 in payments performed autonomously by AI agents on the ledger as of early November. Other networks saw $42.6 million in cumulative x402 stablecoin settlements as of Nov. 9.

In other words, in crypto's two hottest growth areas, XRP is a niche player at best.

Last year, Ripple made a spate of acquisitions to build out its capabilities in crypto custody, trade settlement, and liquidity provision for institutional players. But that's not something likely to make XRP a better buy at this juncture.

In theory, if institutions actually respond to those efforts by working with Ripple, onboarding their capital to the XRPL, and tapping its various services, it probably still wouldn't boost XRP's price much, since XRP isn't even needed to use most of what Ripple offers. And even if Ripple's services did use XRP in some way, its transaction fees are so low that the return to holders wouldn't be meaningful.

Thus, XRP is probably not the best cryptocurrency to buy right now. If the XRPL's performance metrics change, it still might not be enough to change that.

5 essential iPhone security settings that protect you from thieves, spoof callers, and scammers

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Why factory settings aren’t enough to keep your personal data safe

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Securing your digital life can feel overwhelming, but the most effective protections are the ones you can activate in just a few minutes. Your iPhone comes equipped with built-in features engineered to safeguard your identity, shield your finances, and keep your personal information private.

You do not need technical expertise to take advantage of them either, you just need to know where to look. Here are five essential settings that will help protect you and your iPhone from day-to-day threats.

If a thief memorizes your passcode before snatching your phone, that six-digit number used to be enough to reset your master Apple account password and lock you out permanently. Stolen Device Protection eliminates this flaw.

When enabled, your iPhone demands Face ID or Touch ID authenticationfor sensitive actions whenever you are away from familiar locations like home or work. Crucially, it imposes a mandatory one-hour delay before anyone can alter account credentials outside those trusted zones, giving you time to mark the hardware lost via iCloud.

To enable it, go to Settings, tap Face ID & Passcode, and turn on Stolen Device Protection.

Scammers frequently rely on high-pressure calls and spoofed texts to trick you into wiring funds or surrendering credentials. Introduced in iOS 27, Impersonation Risk Detection serves as Apple’s built-in defense against this social engineering.

The system monitors on-device interaction patterns and behavioral cues during high-stakes actions without reading your personal messages, emails, or photos. If you attempt a large transfer or sensitive account change under signs of coercion, your iPhone flags the event and directs supported banking apps to require secondary verification before funds can leave your account.

To activate it, open Settings, tap Privacy & Security, scroll down and select Impersonation Risk Detection, then toggle it on.

Most financial fraud starts with an unsolicited call from someone posing as a bank representative, courier, or tax official. Silence Unknown Callers stops these intrusions before your phone even rings.

To set this up, open Settings, Apps, tap Phone, and check Silence Unknown Callers. Then go to Settings, tap Messages, and toggle on Filter Unknown Senders.

Standard location tracking fails if a thief turns your phone off or the battery drains completely. Expanding your configuration to include the entire Find My network solves this problem.

To turn both on, open Settings, tap your name at the top, select Find My, tap Find My iPhone, and toggle on both Find My network and Send Last Location.

This lets nearby Apple devices locate your iPhone even when it is powered down or offline, while Send Last Location sends one final location ping right before the battery dies.

Leaving your lock screen on its default settings makes it easy for anyone nearby to see private texts, calendar alerts, and one-time bank codes. Tightening what shows up and what works while your phone is locked keeps your personal business to yourself.

To hide your alerts, open Settings, tap Notifications, select Show Previews, and choose When Unlocked.

Next, head to Face ID & Passcode, scroll down to Allow Access When Locked, and switch off Control Center, Wallet, and USB Accessories.

"It's Terrific" - Take-Two Boss Acknowledges Zelda Remake's High Score Ahead Of GTA VI Launch

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

There's been a lot of conversation online about the Zelda remake and Grand Theft Auto going head-to-head this month.

GamesBeat has now asked the Take-Two Interactive boss Strauss Zelnick what he thinks about this and Ocarina of Time's current Metacritic score of 96/100.

He politely responded, saying "it's terrific" and Nintendo should be "very proud of the title".

As for the idea of the two games competing against each other, the CEO of Rockstar's parent company didn't really acknowledge any direct "competition", but more broadly, believes "you compete against everything and nothing" in the entertainment business.

"In the entertainment business, you compete against everything and nothing. Everything because there’s so many ways consumers can spend their time, and nothing because if there’s nothing good out there, people don’t need to consume any of it. It’s like food; like food could be bad, but you still are going to be hungry. But in the case of entertainment, it’s not a must-have; it’s a want to have. So it’s our job to make the best entertainment on earth. When we do that, people will show up for it.”

In his words, if it's the best entertainment, "people will show up for it".

While Zelda's Ocarina remake has done tremendously well for Nintendo, and early reports suggest its sales are off to a positive start in certain locations, Grand Theft Auto VI (at least for now) has been labelled the most anticipated launch of all time.
