Netflix Officially Loses the Streaming War to Prime Video

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Feel free to connect with him or check out his work. He's everywhere — Upwork, YouTube, Spotify, SoundCloud, Collider, LinkedIn, Instagram.

Netflix has poured its massive resources into becoming a powerhouse for anime with its originals, but Prime Video still manages to niche-match and somewhat surpass its anime fanbase in comparison. Anime! Anime! recently published a survey that very narrowly figured out which streaming services the respondents use, and the results have been interesting.

The online Japanese information website published its 2026 oshikatsu reader survey on November 5, and among respondents who use subscription streaming, about 50% cited Prime Video, while roughly 30% cited Netflix. U-NEXT and Hulu were around 10% each, with Disney+ and Anime Store also mentioned.

However, there is a catch to this info from several angles. Firstly, responders could use more than one service, so to bypass that, the survey focused on anime-focused usage instead of subscriptions. Similarly, the published demographics say that the respondents were 65% male and 35% female, with people in their 30s making up about 30% and those around 19 or younger making up about 25%. At the same time, the survey does not reveal the total sample size. So while the numbers should be taken with a pinch of salt, it doesn’t necessarily rule out the idea that Prime Video, in fact, has a large active anime fanbase for obvious reasons.

There's broader 2026 data supporting the idea that Prime Video is extremely competitive with Netflix specifically for anime in Japan. For instance, before this survey, a Media Partners Asia report covering eight Asian markets found that Netflix accounted for about 50% of tracked premium-VOD anime viewing hours across the region in the first half of 2026. But Japan was dramatically closer. In Japan, Prime Video had 43% of anime viewing hours in H2 2025, and by H1 2026, Prime Video and Netflix were tied at approximately 42% each.

That’s directly consistent with Anime! Anime!’s survey’s results. While Netflix has an excellent and enormous originals footprint, Prime Video is a serious rival thanks to its broad mix of anime alongside movies, dramas, and other programming. Not to mention that Japan bundles Prime Video with the wider Amazon Prime membership. This means that someone who has Prime for shipping already has access to its video library.

How to get this Siri camera upgrade for your iPad before its public release

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

You’ll be able to access Visual Intelligence from a new tab in the tablet's Camera app.

Apple users willing to install beta versions of its OS updates can get their hands on upcoming features sooner than most, as long as they're comfortable contending with the possibility of buggy software or apps breaking. And one such feature is the Siri Mode that's already available in the camera app on supported iPhones. It's also coming to compatible iPads as part of iPadOS 27.3 and will let you ask Siri AI questions about things you point your tablet's camera at.

We got our first look at this mode when Apple properly unveiled Siri AI at its Worldwide Developers Conference in June this year. It basically brings the company's Visual Intelligence tool for asking questions from photos to the camera app. But there's one distinguishing feature that's unique to iPads which isn't available on iPhones.

On iPads, Visual Intelligence includes Apple Pencil support. So, much like with Google's Circle to Search feature, you can snap a photo of something with your iPad, use your Apple Pencil to draw a circle around an item and learn more information about the object with the help of Siri AI.

It's worth noting that Visual Intelligence is already available on compatible tablets — iPad Pro and iPad Air with the M1 chip and newer, and iPad mini with the A17 Pro or A20 Pro chipset. As things stand, you can access it in the Camera app by long-pressing the Camera Control button or pressing the Action button if you've set it up to activate the feature (and your iPad has an Action button, of course).

In iPadOS 27.3, you'll have another way to access Visual Intelligence in the Camera app. All you'll have to do is swipe through the options in the viewfinder (i.e. photo, video and portrait modes) until you get to the Siri tab.

The company is expected to release the public version of iPadOS 27.3 in December. However, you can try it on your tablet right now, because Apple released the first beta in early November.

To install the iPadOS 27.3 beta, open the Settings app on your iPad, then tap General > Software Update. If you haven't already done so, you'll need to enable beta updates. Tap the Beta Updates option, then iPadOS 27 Public Beta. After you go back to the Software Update tab, you should be able to install the iPadOS 27.3 beta.

Before you install the beta, it's best to create a full backup of your iPad using iCloud, a Mac or a Windows PC. Beta builds aren't the finished product, meaning they're more susceptible to bugs and errors. Backing up your data should be standard practice anyway, but it's especially advisable to do that before installing a beta version of an operating system, just to make sure your files and other information stay safe.

If you don't feel comfortable enough to install a iPadOS beta, that's perfectly fine! The public version of the OS update is likely coming soon. You can check out the Siri tab on your iPad's Camera app at that point.

‘De Gaulle,’ ‘La Gradiva’ and ‘Alice: Pioneer Of Cinema’ Among American French Film Fest Winners

The American French Film Festival announced the winners of this year’s awards during the closing ceremony on Nov. 8.

The films were voted on by audiences attending the festival, members of the Los Angeles Film Critics Association, a jury of entertainment professionals and a student jury consisting of high school and college-age participants from the TAFFF High School Screenings Program.

“De Gaulle,” written by Antonin Baudry and Bérénice Vila, directed by Antonin Baudry and Niels Schneider, Benoît Magimel and Anamaria Vartolomei, took home the Audience Award and top honor for best film.

The Critics Award for best film was given to “La Gradiva.” Written by Marine Atlan and Anne Brouillet, the film is directed by Marine Atlan and stars Colas Quignard, Antonia Buresi, Mitia Capellier-Audat and Suzanne Gerin.

“A Place For Her” received the First Feature Award; “Whispers In The Woods” received the Documentary Film Award; “The Green Eyes” received American Students Award; “Alice: Pioneer Of Cinema” received the Audience Series Award; “Swapped” received the Jury Series Award; “Diego” received the Audience Short Film Award; “God is Shy” received the Jury Short Film Award.

The Distribution Collective will host the annual Diversity and Inclusion Film Festival at the Lincoln Center campus from Nov. 13 to Nov. 15.

Special programming will include a Universal Studio Group panel on Nov. 14, featuring Hanako Greensmith (“Chicago Fire”), Zeeko Zaki (“FBI”) and David Ajala (“Law & Order”), who will discuss carrying forward the legacy of Wolf Entertainment’s iconic television franchises while introducing new dimensions to beloved series.

Standard Chartered Expands Prime Brokerage Partnership With 24X to Cover BTC and ETH

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Institutional investors will be able to access Bitcoin and Ether trading through an expanded relationship between 24X and Standard Chartered. In a November 10 announcement, 24X Bermuda said the banking group will extend its prime-brokerage role from foreign-exchange products to digital assets on 24X’s multi-asset technology platform.

TL;DR: 24X says Standard Chartered is expanding its prime-brokerage relationship to cover BTC and ETH. The arrangement is aimed at institutional counterparties accessing crypto and FX through one execution infrastructure. It changes the trading and counterparty workflow for eligible participants; it is not a retail exchange rollout.

The company’s stated model allows an institutional participant to face Standard Chartered as its central counterparty while using 24X for execution, matching, liquidity configuration and straight-through processing. That separation matters. The participant’s commercial relationship and credit exposure are handled differently from the technology that matches orders.

Traditional foreign exchange and spot cryptocurrency trading do not have identical market structures or risk profiles. FX markets have established bank relationships, while crypto markets operate around the clock and rely on a patchwork of exchanges, brokers and custody providers. An integrated platform can simplify some operational tasks, although it still requires clients to understand the product-specific terms.

The release says 24X already offered FX, non-deliverable forwards and spot services. BTC and ETH are the additional digital-asset instruments named in the expanded partnership. It would be misleading to claim the agreement immediately covers every token, derivative or jurisdiction.

A recognized bank acting as a central relationship may appeal to institutions that want more familiar onboarding, credit arrangements and oversight. It does not remove Bitcoin volatility, market liquidity problems or the possibility of disruptions in the surrounding infrastructure. Nor should the word ‘prime’ be used as a shortcut for saying all trades are insured or guaranteed.

Institutions will need to evaluate exposure limits, settlement timing, custody arrangements, collateral terms and what happens during market stress. The distinction between an execution venue and the organization responsible for a trading obligation is especially important for crypto, where the history of market failures has made counterparty concentration a persistent concern.

NewsBTC previously looked at Ripple Prime’s effort to bring traditional markets into 24-hour trading workflows. That is a distinct product and operator, but it reflects the same strategic demand: customers increasingly want to manage digital and conventional exposures without jumping between disconnected systems.

The announcement establishes the partnership expansion, but it does not provide a full ledger of live client trades, volumes or fee terms. Commercial success depends on whether eligible counterparties find the combined trading and brokerage model attractive enough to move business from existing venues.

As tokenized securities and digital assets enter more established markets, investors may become less focused on whether a blockchain is present and more concerned with the legal machinery behind settlement. Our report on the NYSE’s tokenized securities infrastructure partners describes why those arrangements matter. For 24X, the next proof point will be active institutional adoption rather than the breadth of the announcement alone.

Calciomercato no stop - Indiscrezioni, trattative e retroscena del 10 novembre

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

INTER, CALHANOGLU PUÒ RINNOVARE. LAVORI IN CORSO PER IL NUOVO ACCORDO CON THURAM. CISSE RIMANE AL MILAN, RIFIUTATE DUE PROPOSTE. COMO, IDEA MALO GUSTO CON I BUONI RAPPORTI INSIEME AL CHELSEA. JUVENTUS, NON BASTANO GLI ATTACCANTI CHE HA SPALLETTI: PENSIERO FISSO CON SORLOTH. TUTTI GLI OBIETTIVI DEL NAPOLI PER RIPARTIRE A GENNAIO, ALLEGRI HA UNA PRIORITÀ. ROMA, ENDRICK CHIODO FISSO E SPUNTA BALDE COME IDEA DEL MERCATO INVERNALE.

Il tema del rinnovo di contratto di Hakan Calhanoglu è tornato improvvisamente d'attualità in casa Inter, la volontà comune è quella di proseguire insieme e tentare di chiudere l'accordo al più presto, persino entro Natale se si incastreranno tutti i tasselli. Si tratta di un segnale distensivo fondamentale dopo un periodo turbolento iniziato ad agosto: durante la tournée australiana, infatti, il centrocampista turco aveva manifestato qualche perplessità sulla mancanza di contatti per il prolungamento e il timore di non essere più considerato centrale nelle strategie del club, pur ribadendo il forte legame con Milano, l'Inter e i tifosi e forte della fiducia incondizionata del tecnico Cristian Chivu. Ritrovata la serenità ed eseguito il primo passo con la riapertura del dialogo, il vero nodo resta tuttavia di natura economica. Attualmente legato ai nerazzurri fino al 2027 a circa 6,5 milioni netti a stagione, Calhanoglu punta a un nuovo biennale con un ingaggio da top player; dal canto suo, la società mira a ridurre la quota fissa degli stipendi tenendo conto dell'età e dei recenti guai fisici del classe '94. La trattativa rimane dunque aperta e richiederà la giusta intesa tra le parti per arrivare alla fumata bianca.

In casa Inter si lavora per blindare Marcus Thuram: il club nerazzurro ha infatti avviato i primi contatti per prolungare l'accordo con l'attaccante francese, attualmente in scadenza nel 2028, con l'obiettivo di estenderlo fino al 2031. La trattativa si preannuncia tuttavia in salita per motivi economici: la società guidata in panchina da Cristian Chivu vorrebbe proporre una ristrutturazione dell'ingaggio basata su una quota fissa da 4,5-5 milioni di euro integrata da premi e benefit, a fronte dello stipendio attuale da 6,5 milioni che con i bonus sfiora gli 8 milioni a stagione. Una formula che per ora non convince la punta, decisa a non ridimensionare le proprie condizioni contrattuali pur ribadendo la priorità assoluta di vestire la maglia dell'Inter, pur in presenza dei fari puntati da diversi club esteri per l'estate. La forte spinta dei dirigenti verso il rinnovo è legata a doppio filo al rendimento sul campo: dall'infortunio di Lautaro Martinez, Thuram si è preso il peso dell'attacco collezionando quattro gol e due assist in sei gare di campionato, confermandosi elemento imprescindibile nello scacchiere tattico nerazzurro.

Alphadjo Cisse non ha nascosto l'entusiasmo per il suo travolgente inizio di stagione con la maglia del Milan: l'attaccante rossonero ha confidato come la tripletta messa a segno in Europa League sia stata la conferma di poter stare a questi livelli, vivendola non come un punto d'arrivo ma come una promessa mantenuta al bambino che era, consapevole dei propri mezzi e molto esigente con se stesso nonostante il recente lungo infortunio e la giovane età. Una fiducia ricambiata appieno dalla società di via Aldo Rossi che, a pochi mesi dall'arrivo del giocatore, è già al lavoro con i suoi agenti per blindarlo e respingere le sirene di mercato, in particolare quelle del Brighton che sta bussando alla porta con offerte milionarie: l'obiettivo del Milan è infatti quello di adeguare le condizioni contrattuali di Cisse, portando l'ingaggio attuale da circa 800 mila euro al doppio e prolungando la scadenza oltre l'attuale termine del 30 giugno 2030.

Il Como potrebbe inserirsi nella corsa per Malo Gusto: il club lariano, rivelazione della Champions League, avrebbe messo nel mirino il terzino destro classe 2003 del Chelsea, il quale sarebbe pronto a valutare un cambio di scenario nella prossima finestra di mercato pur di trovare più spazio in prima squadra e difendere il proprio ruolo centrale in ottica nazionale francese. Dal canto loro, i Blues non avrebbero chiuso la porta a un addio già a gennaio, complice una discontinuità testimoniata dalle zero presenze da titolare nelle ultime sei uscite di Premier League per l'ex Lione, arrivato a Londra nel 2023. Per accaparrarsi il talento transalpino, apprezzato per giovane età, spinta sulla fascia e ampi margini di crescita, il Como dovrà tuttavia superare la agguerrita concorrenza internazionale di Atletico Madrid, Milan e Lipsia, pronte a scatenare un'asta qualora i dirigenti inglesi aprano definitivamente alla cessione a titolo definitivo o in prestito.

La Juventus continua a valutare opzioni per il mercato di riparazione di gennaio ed è pronta a tornare alla carica per Alexander Sorloth: nonostante i lievi segnali di crescita mostrati da Nick Woltemade e Randal Kolo Muani, i bianconeri intendono regalare al tecnico Luciano Spalletti una punta forte fisicamente e abile ad attaccare la profondità, riallacciando i contatti per il centravanti norvegese dell'Atletico Madrid già concretamente seguito a giugno con un viaggio in Norvegia del direttore sportivo Marco Ottolini. Pur apprezzato da Diego Simeone dopo aver superato i guai fisici di inizio stagione, l'attaccante ex Lipsia non è considerato intoccabile dai Colchoneros e vorrebbe maggior spazio; forte degli ottimi rapporti tra i club - e con la posizione di Jonathan David, attualmente a Madrid in prestito dalla Juve, che potrebbe incidere nelle dinamiche -, la società torinese mantiene attiva la pista, pur monitorando profili alternativi come Gianluca Scamacca, Mateo Retegui e Folarin Balogun, quest'ultimo già proposto in estate da Jorge Mendes.

Il Napoli ha già iniziato a pianificare le strategie per la sessione invernale di calciomercato: la dirigenza azzurra è al lavoro per mettere a disposizione del tecnico Massimiliano Allegri quattro rinforzi mirati - nello specifico un centravanti, un esterno d'attacco e due centrocampisti -, un piano che potrebbe essere accompagnato anche da alcune uscite per ridisegnare l'organico in vista della seconda parte di stagione. Per l'attacco il profilo in cima alle preferenze è quello del ventunenne Gustavo Varela, protagonista di un avvio di campionato straordinario con la maglia del Monza che lo vede guidare la classifica marcatori di Serie A con nove reti (undici totali in stagione) ed essere premiato con la chiamata della nazionale portoghese, mentre sull'esterno resta vivo l'interesse per il ventiduenne Jamie Bynoe-Gittens, in uscita dal Chelsea e gradito anche per questioni di lista. Infine, per il reparto mediano i fari sono puntati sul giovane talento del Sassuolo Darryl Bakola, che compirà 19 anni a fine novembre, ma il vero sogno e priorità assoluta rimane Mamadou Coulibaly, classe 2004 del Monaco, per il quale tuttavia la valutazione di circa 40 milioni di euro pretesa dai francesi rende la trattativa assai complessa pur senza scoraggiare i dirigenti partenopei.

Il futuro di Endrick al Real Madrid si trova a un bivio in vista della sessione invernale di calciomercato: il ventenne, attaccante brasiliano, sta valutando seriamente di lasciare i blancos a gennaio per ritrovare continuità ed evitare di rallentare la propria crescita. Nonostante l'ottimo rapporto con José Mourinho, che in estate ne aveva bloccato il trasferimento alla Roma, la punta ha collezionato soltanto 140 minuti spalmati su 7 presenze, ripercorrendo l'inizio della scorsa stagione prima che il prestito al Lione gli permettesse di esplodere con 8 gol e 7 assist in 21 gare e conquistare la chiamata al Mondiale con la Seleçao del CT Carlo Ancelotti. Sulle sue tracce resta vigile la Roma, ma la concorrenza più agguerrita è quella dello Sporting guidato proprio da Paulo Fonseca, il tecnico che lo aveva valorizzato in Francia e che saprebbe offrirgli garanzie d'impiego, la vetrina della Champions League e un ambientamento facilitato dalla lingua; dal canto suo, la dirigenza del Real Madrid non intende ostacolare la partenza per tutelare un investimento superiore agli 80 milioni ed evitare svalutazioni, mentre dal ritiro del Brasile lo stesso Endrick ha ribadito con maturo affetto la propria priorità, dichiarando di apprezzare la vicinanza umana di Mourinho ma di voler solo giocare e dare un reale impulso alla propria carriera.

In casa Roma l'avvio di campionato ha certificato ambizioni da scudetto: dopo undici giornate la squadra ha dimostrato di avere i numeri per lottare fino in fondo e la finestra invernale di calciomercato rappresenta l'occasione perfetta per puntellare la rosa a disposizione di Gian Piero Gasperini con un paio di innesti di spessore. Tra i profili finiti nel mirino del direttore sportivo Tony D’Amico c'è Alejandro Balde, esterno sinistro classe 2003 in forza al Barcellona; il club catalano potrebbe aprire alla cessione già a gennaio per fare cassa e investire su un difensore centrale, vera priorità del tecnico Hansi Flick. Incassato il pieno gradimento di Gasperini, lo scoglio principale per i giallorossi rimane la formula dell'operazione - con l'ipotesi di un prestito oneroso con obbligo di riscatto condizionato alla qualificazione in Champions League -, resa complessa da un'alta valutazione del cartellino e da un ingaggio di rilievo. A complicare ulteriormente i piani della Roma c'è poi l'affollata concorrenza internazionale scatenata dall'apertura dei blaugrana: in Premier League si muovono Liverpool, Aston Villa, Manchester United, Manchester City e Tottenham, in Ligue 1 c'è il Monaco, mentre in Italia anche la Juventus valuta l'opzione nell'eventualità di un addio di Andrea Cambiaso, rendendo il terzino spagnolo uno dei pezzi pregiati più contesi della imminente sessione di mercato.

ANTONIO CONTE SORPRENDE: PROCURA A ZAHAVI, VUOLE LO UNITED. LINDELOF RINNOVA CON L'ASTON VILLA, MANCA SOLO L'UFFICIALITÀ PER L'ESONERO DI CAHUZAC DAL LENS, VAN DIJK NON PENSA AL FUTURO PER ORA.

La panchina di Michael Carrick al Manchester United appare ormai sul punto di crollare: secondo le ultime indiscrezioni, l'esonero del tecnico inglese è considerato una semplice questione di tempo nei prossimi giorni, dato che il rocambolesco 4-4 strappato in extremis a Old Trafford contro l'Aston Villa non è bastato a raddrizzare la rotta né a sanare il feeling incrinato con lo spogliatoio. Mentre le quotazioni di Mauricio Pochettino continuavano a salire - con l'attuale commissario tecnico degli Stati Uniti che avrebbe già dato la propria disponibilità a raccogliere il testimone, forte di una clausola rescissoria da 9 milioni di euro per liberarsi dal contratto fino al 2030 con la selezione americana e di una residenza familiare a Londra -, il quadro si è improvvisamente complicato con l'inserimento a sorpresa di Antonio Conte. Il 57enne, allenatore leccese, libero dopo l'addio al Napoli a fine scorsa stagione, si sarebbe infatti affidato al potente agente Pini Zahavi con l'obiettivo specifico di farsi avanti per la guida dei Red Devils; in attesa di capire se la dirigenza di Old Trafford affonderà il colpo prima del doppio impegno di Pochettino nei quarti di Concacaf Nations League contro Haiti, la corsa alla successione di Carrick resta più intricata che mai.

L'Aston Villa blinda Victor Lindelof. Il club inglese ha annunciato di aver esercitato l'opzione presente nel contratto del difensore svedese, prolungando l'accordo fino al 2028. Arrivato a Villa Park nel 2025, l'ex Manchester United ha avuto un impatto immediato, diventando uno dei punti di riferimento della squadra. Nel suo primo anno con la maglia dei Villans è stato protagonista anche nella conquista dell'Europa League, giocando un ruolo importante nella finale vinta 3-0 contro il Friburgo a Istanbul. Lindelöf ha finora disputato 43 partite con la maglia dell'Aston Villa e si è rapidamente affermato al centro della difesa grazie alla sua esperienza e alla capacità di guidare il reparto arretrato. Il classe 1994 continua inoltre a essere anche un elemento fondamentale della nazionale svedese, di cui è il capitano. Con il rinnovo fino al 2028, il club di Birmingham conferma la volontà di puntare ancora sulle sue qualità.

Il Lens si appresta a cambiare nuovamente guida tecnica: nelle prossime ore la società francese ufficializzerà l'esonero di Yannick Cahuzac, maturato dopo la riunione dirigenziale di lunedì sera e motivato da un avvio di stagione condellato da risultati deludenti e devastanti sul piano psicologico. Figura emblematica del club, Cahuzac lascerà definitivamente i Sang et Or senza essere ricollocato in altri ruoli societari dopo un'avventura durata appena due mesi: subentrato il 21 settembre a Dino Toppmoller in seguito al ko con il Monaco (1-2), il tecnico ha visto segnato il proprio destino dal recente k.o. interno contro il Marsiglia (1-2), arrivato a pochi giorni dal pesantissimo 1-9 incassato in Champions League contro il Como, sconfitta che rappresenta la peggiore di sempre per una squadra transalpina nelle coppe europee. Per la successione la dirigenza sta valutando tre profili e tra questi spunta la clamorosa candidatura di Pierre Sage: andato via in estate, l'ex allenatore si trova attualmente in bilico sulla panchina del Crystal Palace, con un esonero imminente in Premier League che potrebbe trasformarlo nella soluzione immediata per restituire stabilità all'ambiente.

Virgil van Dijk non teme di finire in scadenza con il Liverpool al termine della stagione, nonostante i suoi 35 anni. "Se si prolunga, tanto meglio. Altrimenti, andrò altrove. Non c'è nulla da segnalare per il momento, ma questo non significa che sono in panico. Mentalmente sto bene; fisicamente ho avuto un piccolo problema in queste ultime due settimane, ma sto facendo tutto il possibile per essere presente sul campo". Qualsiasi possibilità per il difensore olandese, che a parametro zero farebbe certamente gola a chiunque.

Hasan Piker’s $72.5M Defamation Suit Over Lynching Quotes Should Be Tossed Out, Scott Jennings Says; Statements Are “Literally True,” CNN Contributor Claims

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Executive Editor, Legal, Labor & Politics

The midterm elections are over, but the multi-million defamation battle between Hasan Piker and Scott Jennings over what the progressive streamer said about lynching in America 2026, and the context in which he said it, is just getting started.

In a 15-page response to Piker’s $72.5 million lawsuit of last month, the conservative CNN contributor does whatever the double-down version of doubling-down is. To that, Jennings wants a federal judge throw Piker’s action out.

“The Court should dismiss the complaint for failure to state a claim upon which relief can be granted,” says the motion placed in the DC court docket Tuesday by Jennings’ attorneys Benjamin Chew, Nina Franco and Peter Baumhart. Watch on Deadline Related Stories News House Blocks Effort To Impeach Secretary Of Defense Pete Hegseth News Pete Hegseth Defends Tenure, Lavishes Praise On Trump & Calls Impeachment Threat "Sabotage" In Rare Pentagon Press Conference

Describing the politically influential Piker as “an internet personality and provocateur” who “makes his living courting controversy—to put it mildly,” the filing doesn’t note the irony of such a statement coming from reps for MAGAman Jennings, who many would say does exactly the same thing, and did it in this case. In fact, Piker’s Meier Watkins Phillips Pusch lawyers alleging in their initial October 27 filing that regular CNN NewsNight panelist Jennings is himself all about “political controversy.”

It is in that vein that Piker’s attorneys particularly pinpoint Jennings’ August 11 podcast where they assert the Trump press secretary auditioning pundit “falsely told his listeners that Mr. Piker had a ‘plan of returning our country to lynchings …then he falsely told his social media followers that Mr. Piker says the USA should resume lynchings against black people.'”

Which is why Piker, who has subsequently said he may pursue similar methods against others he believes take his words out of context, wants $72.5 million bucks from Jennings.

Seeking an oral argument ASAP before Judge Amit P. Mehta on today’s motion, Jennings team admit that “in August 2026, Mr. Jennings played on his political discussion program a video of Mr. Piker saying these things.” Noting the video in question was off a social media site attributed to “Senate Republicans,” they describe that “Mr. Piker now alleges that “the video—which Mr. Jennings did not create, edit, or manipulate and had no reason to doubt based on the source of the video and the totality of what is known about Mr. Piker’s general worldview—was defamatory, as was Mr. Jennings’s accompanying commentary.”

Basically, Jennings’ argument is that on the August 11 edition of The Scott Jennings Show he took at face value what he saw and heard Piker utter on the supposedly GOP connected feed.

Instead, declaring there is no “actual malice” alleged by Piker, out of that commentary, Jennings’ attorneys in today’s parsed response say “the statements in the NRSC Video are literally true—there is and can be no dispute that Mr. Piker said what he is on camera saying.” They add: “The dispute is over whether those statements reflect what he really meant, which is not sufficiently verifiable to be actionable as defamation. On either or both of these independently sufficient grounds, Mr. Piker’s claims should be dismissed.”

Again, Jennings appears to be affirming that whether or not he disagrees or agrees with Piker politically, Piker is a fella who commonly says outrageous things and what was on this video tracked with that stance — hence his reaction.

Maintaining “the first factor—’the common usage or meaning of the allegedly defamatory words’—is somewhat ill-fitted to the facts of this case,” the lawyers for the former Mitch McConnell aide propose “the proper focus of the analysis is therefore not on the words Mr. Jennings used, but on the premise implicit in his commentary that Mr. Piker’s statements were genuine. That premise, because it is unspoken, necessarily does not admit of the sort of linguistic analysis called for by the first factor.”

One factor to consider: even as this charged suit moves through the courts, Jennings continues to appear on CNN talking politics.

L'Inter perde i pezzi: dopo Bonny, si fermano anche Thuram e Stankovic. Chivu preoccupato

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

È un periodo clamorosamente sfortunato per l'Inter di Cristian Chivu. Neanche il tempo di festeggiare la vittoria per 2-0 contro il Como che i nerazzurri devono fare i conti con gli infortuni di Ange-Yoan Bonny, Marcus Thuram e Aleksandar Stankovic, quest'ultimi due resi noti nella giornata di martedì: con Lautaro Martinez ancora ai box, la speranza del tecnico rumeno è di recuperare almeno il Toro e un altro interprete per non rischiare di rimanere con il solo Esposito in attacco.

Thuram ha riportato una tensione al muscolo ischiocrurale destro e non sembra preoccupare più di tanto per la sfida contro l'Atalanta, in programma alla ripresa. I tempi di recupero saranno chiariti in seguito, ma l'ennesimo infortunio non lascia dormire sonni tranquilli all'Inter, che dovrebbe comunque riabbracciare pure Stones e Jones.

Bitcoin devs merge 3 new BIPs, and transaction fees could plummet

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Bitcoin proposals could cut fees by up to 27% through signature aggregation, with wallet upgrades and network activation still required.

Bitcoin's proposal repository added three draft specifications on Nov. 2-4 that could lower fees for payments assembled jointly by multiple users and for consolidating many inputs into one output.

BIP458 merged on Nov. 2, BIP459 on Nov. 3, and BIP460 on Nov. 4. Their mechanism combines the signatures attached to transaction inputs, the coins being spent.

Half-aggregation combines independently produced signatures without an interactive signing session: n signatures of 64 bytes become 32(n+1) bytes. Full aggregation requires coordinated signing but produces a single 64-byte signature for all aggregated signatures in a group.

For ten signatures, that means shrinking 640 bytes to 352 or 64 bytes, respectively. That's a 45% and 90% reduction in signature payload, before encoding overhead. The reduction in the whole transaction's fee would be much smaller.

Bitcoin fees are commonly expressed per virtual byte (vB). Under Bitcoin's weight rules, virtual size is transaction weight divided by four, rounded up.

Hypothetical examples put all inputs in one eligible aggregation group and use default signing settings, 34-byte output scripts and no optional extra witness data, or annex. Transaction overhead, witness encoding, and the group's marker byte are included.

A joint transaction with ten inputs and ten outputs would shrink from 1,016 vB to 944 vB with half-aggregation or 872 vB with full aggregation, lowering its fee by about 7.1% or 14.2%. At an illustrative 10 satoshis per vB, the savings would be 720 or 1,440 satoshis.

Consolidation benefits more in the second example of 100 inputs combined into one output. Its size falls from 5,804 vB to 5,012 vB with half-aggregation or 4,220 vB with full aggregation, cutting fees by approximately 13.6% or 27.3%.

Ten signatures can shrink by 90% with full aggregation. Compare how much the whole fee could fall in two hypothetical transactions.

10 inputs, 10 outputs. Many outputs leave less of the transaction available for signature compression.

100 inputs, 1 output. Signature-heavy consolidation has a larger percentage fee benefit under these assumptions.

The input/output mix changes the fee benefit. These Draft proposals still require a soft fork and wallet support.

Hypothetical examples: all inputs eligible in one group, default sighashes, no annex, 34-byte output scripts. Blue includes the group marker in transaction panels; top section is signature payload alone. Not current network averages or available discounts. Activation parameters remain unset.

Original calculations from BIP460 witness forms and BIP141/144 encoding. Base=10+41n+43m bytes; W0=4B+2+66n, Wh=4B+2+2n+32(n+1)+1, Wf=4B+2+2n+65. vB=ceil(W/4). Bars scale by weight; labels are rounded vB. Fee savings=(v0-v)/v0 at a fixed rate, rounded to 0.1%.

Hypothetical examples: all inputs eligible in one group, default sighashes, no annex, 34-byte output scripts. Blue includes the group marker in transaction panels; top section is signature payload alone. Not current network averages or available discounts. Activation parameters remain unset.

BIP460 leaves activation parameters unspecified, and its proposed witness version, currently v2, needs coordination with other proposals.

Wallets would need to support the new output type, as existing Taproot outputs would not automatically gain aggregation, which is limited to direct signature-based or key-path spends.

Spends that execute scripts, known as script-path spends, cannot join aggregation groups, and eligible inputs can opt out.

Full aggregation needs a stateful two-round signing protocol, and reusing secret nonces can expose signing keys, making hardware and air-gapped support demanding. Half-aggregation avoids that joint signing session, although collaborative transaction construction still requires agreement.

CFTC warns Kalshi of imminent charges over notification of contracts: Politico

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The prediction markets operator says the issue involves procedural errors affecting a handful of contracts, many of which weren’t live, according to Politico.

Prediction markets operator Kalshi is being warned of imminent charges from the US Commodity Futures Trading Commission over whether the operator properly notified the agency about new products, Politico reported Tuesday.

The Commodity Futures Trading Commission is investigating notifications involving dozens of products, the report said, citing a person with direct knowledge of the probe. The agency’s private notification to Kalshi suggests that charges could follow.

The investigation has been underway for months, the person said.

Speaking to Politico, Kalshi spokesperson Elisabeth Diana described the matter as a procedural error involving submissions for a handful of contracts, many of which never went live.

“We are already addressing the issue,” she added.

The potential enforcement action comes as the CFTC fights to establish its exclusive authority over prediction markets, including by challenging state efforts to regulate Kalshi.

Separately, Kalshi is backing the regulator in a separate legal battle over its authority. On Monday, the company joined Coinbase and four other firms in seeking permission to file a brief supporting the CFTC’s treatment of perpetual futures tied to individual US stocks.

Italia, Zaniolo lascia Coverciano e spiega perché. Liberali e Cisse parlano in conferenza

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

L'Italia di Roberto Mancini è quella dei giovani, di Mattia Liberali e Alphadjo Cisse, due debuttanti in azzurro che hanno condiviso insieme l'esperienza dello scorso anno al Catanzaro. Non è invece la Nazionale di Nicolò Zaniolo, che ha lasciato il ritiro per questioni legate alla sua vita e non rese note dal classe '99 e dalla FIGC.

Il numero 10 dell'Udinese ha chiarito sui social la sua decisione: "Non è stato facile, è dovuta a motivi strettamente personali. Vestire l'azzurro è sempre stato motivo d'orgoglio. Grazie al Mister, allo staff e ai compagni per la vicinanza. Chiedo solo che la mia sfera privata venga rispettata, senza dare credito a ricostruzioni che non mi appartengono. Questa maglia la porto nel cuore, e continuerò a lavorare ogni giorno per tornare a indossarla".

Il fantasista del Como è tornato sul suo addio al Milan in conferenza: "Non sono rammaricato, le scelte bisogna farle con sicurezza e non bisogna guardarsi indietro. Non è stato facile, ma è sempre importante guardare avanti. Nel mio nuovo club sta andando molto bene e poi mi sto trovando molto bene anche con il gruppo, Fabregas mi sta aiutando tanto, mi ha dato molta fiducia fin dal primo momento".

Chi sta facendo ancora meglio è proprio il classe 2006 del Milan, capocannoniere dei rossoneri: "Mi ispiro a Balotelli, in Nazionale è sempre stato un riferimento per me. La pressione mi piace e credo di avere le spalle larghe per reggerle. Qui ci sono giocatori importanti, c'è chi ha vinto l'Europeo e bisogna avere rispetto. Bisogna fare le cose un passo alla volta, il primo pensiero è giocare, per la titolarità si vedrà". Chiosa finale sull'obiettivo Scudetto: "Al Milan vincere è la priorità, qualsiasi competizione. Vincere è la normalità e non si può trascurare alcuna competizione, devi vincere tutto".

Can a bird flu vaccine keep up with a virus that keeps changing?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A computer-designed bird flu vaccine protected mice against three H5N1 strains, offering early evidence of broader protection before human trials.

Governments hold doses of bird flu vaccine in storage for the day the virus starts spreading between people.

Each of those vaccines was built around a single virus sample. H5 bird flu, meanwhile, keeps splitting into branches that look different to the immune system.

A Cambridge team built a rival vaccine, with active ingredients drawn up by computer instead of copied from one virus.

Researchers gave it to mice, then exposed separate groups to three H5N1 viruses spanning two decades of evolution.

Every one of those mice lived. Half the mice given the stockpile-style vaccine died after exposure to the clade 1 virus.

The study was led by Jonathan Heeney, a professor of research in vaccinology in the Department of Veterinary Medicine at the University of Cambridge.

The vaccine comes out of the university spin-out DIOSynVax, where he and several co-authors are employees or shareholders.

The tests were in mice and ferrets, and no one has given this vaccine to a person.

In an interview with Earth.com, Heeney said vaccine design has spent most of a century looking the wrong way.

“For more than 70 years, vaccines against rapidly evolving viruses have largely been designed by looking backwards at the pathogen that already exists,” he said.

Bird flu now turns up in wild birds, poultry and mammals on nearly every continent. The H5 family has broken into branches that evolve on their own in Asia, Europe, Africa and the Americas.

How dangerous those branches are varies sharply.

The clade 2.3.4.4b viruses spreading in the Americas have caused 97 known human cases and three deaths, and most of those infections were mild.

These viruses have also spread through dairy herds.

The 2.3.2.1e viruses circulating in Southeast Asia have killed more than half the people with reported infections over the past two years.

The bird flu vaccines in national stockpiles are matched to single virus samples, picked through the World Health Organization’s influenza program.

Those vaccines haven’t been widely tested against the other H5 branches now circulating, the authors wrote.

“These vaccines are based on single viral isolates, whereas H5Nx viruses have continued to evolve and diversify into multiple lineages around the world,” Heeney told Earth.com.

“In many cases, viral evolution has outpaced the strains on which those stockpiled vaccines were originally based.”

The part of a vaccine that teaches the immune system has a name: the antigen.

Rather than lift one from a single virus, the team pooled the genetic sequences of H5 viruses collected worldwide by surveillance programs.

Software then drew up antigens carrying the features shared across the whole group.

The vaccine carries mRNA instructions for making three designed antigens.

One is based on the outer protein the virus uses to get into a cell, and a second on the protein it uses to get back out.

The third is a small protein that helps rouse the immune response.

The team screened the candidates in cells, then packaged mRNA encoding the selected antigens inside tiny fat particles, using the same delivery approach as mRNA COVID-19 vaccines.

Heeney said the design looks ahead instead of back.

He noted that his platform uses computational biology to anticipate viral evolution and design broadly protective vaccine antigens before the next outbreak occurs.

The team immunized mice, then exposed them to H5N1 viruses from three branches that have all infected people: clade 1, clade 2.3.2.1e and clade 2.3.4.4b.

All mice given the designed vaccine survived exposure to their assigned H5N1 virus, with no weight loss or signs of illness.

Four days after exposure, no virus could be recovered from their lungs or spleens. The stockpile-style vaccine matched to 2.3.4.4b protected fully against its own matched virus.

Against the 2.3.2.1e virus, 67% of those mice survived, and against clade 1, 50% did.

Blood tests pointed the same way. Antibodies from the designed vaccine blocked lab-made stand-ins for eleven H5 branches.

Each stockpile-style vaccine covered only its own corner of the family.

Ferrets catch and spread flu much as people do, making them an important model for testing influenza vaccines.

The team exposed them to a 2.3.4.4b H5N1 taken from an Italian chicken. In the entry protein, it differs from the stockpile vaccine’s strain by four amino acids.

Every unvaccinated ferret died or reached the humane endpoint by day eight. So did half of those given the stockpile-style vaccine.

All ferrets given the lower dose of the designed vaccine survived without weight loss or fever. One ferret given the higher dose reached the humane endpoint.

The oddity sits in the bloodwork. On the day of exposure, those ferrets scored below the antibody level flu researchers treat as protective against that virus.

Most survived despite those low antibody readings.

Something other than that one measure was carrying the protection, the authors wrote, most likely antibodies against the exit protein working alongside antibodies against the entry protein.

The comparison is not clean, and the authors wrote as much.

Their vaccine is mRNA, while the benchmark is a killed whole virus, so dose, formulation and delivery all differ along with the antigen design.

Comparing both antigen designs using the same mRNA platform would help isolate the effect of antigen design.

The mice also got a single dose of the stockpile-style comparator, a benchmark taken from older work.

And the designed vaccine’s edge over the stockpile shots can’t yet be pinned on the antigen design alone.

Nobody has measured whether any of this holds in people.

The next steps are manufacturing the vaccine to meet regulatory standards and a first trial in humans, Heeney said, and DIOSynVax is raising money to pay for them.

The designed antigen sequences are patented.

What the virus does in the meantime is the part nobody controls. “It’s when bird flu adapts to live in the airways of mammals that we start to get really nervous,” Heeney said.

That is the shift researchers watch for in dairy cows and pigs, and the reason a bird flu vaccine that protects against multiple branches could be valuable.

The full study was published in the journal Cell Reports Medicine.

He has covered everything from Marvel to the Oscars, and Marvel at the Oscars. He also writes obsessively about the box office, charting the many hits and misses that are released weekly, and how their commercial performance shapes public perception. In his time at Collider, he has also helped drive diversity by writing stories about the multiple Indian film industries, with a goal of introducing audiences to a whole new world of cinema.

A couple of years ago, A24 announced its intention to level up and challenge some of the bigger studios in the business. The outfit had spent a decade in the trenches of indie cinema, occasionally delivering a breakout horror hit. Only one of its movies in that first decade — Everything Everywhere All at Once — cracked the coveted $100 million mark worldwide. However, following a major fundraising round, the studio began to release bigger movies that delivered greater hauls at the box office. This year has been particularly successful, with a string of films across genres exceeding expectations. Now, the most unexpected hit of the bunch is nearing a major box-office milestone.

A24's transition to mid-budget films began with Civil War, the dystopian thriller directed by Alex Garland, which generated nearly $130 million worldwide against a reported budget of $50 million. Civil War was followed by Marty Supreme, which grossed nearly $200 million worldwide against a reported budget of $70 million. There were also a couple of major underperformers during this phase, such as Ari Aster's Beau is Afraid and Benny Safdie's The Smashing Machine.

However, things got back on track in 2026, beginning with The Drama. Starring Zendaya and Robert Pattinson, the film grossed more than $130 million worldwide against a reported budget of $28 million. Pattinson also headlined the psychological thriller Primetime, which generated nearly $50 million domestically against a reported budget of $14 million. It was this summer that A24 delivered its biggest-ever hit, the horror film Backrooms, which grossed $400 million worldwide against a reported budget of under $10 million.

Your nemesis is not after money, territory or revenge. He is after a demonstration, and you are the person he has chosen to prove his point on.

Your nemesis respects you, which sounds flattering until you work out what it means. He chose you because nobody else was worth his time.

Your nemesis has no personal interest in you whatsoever, and that is precisely what makes him unsurvivable. You are not a rival. You are a stop on the way.

Co-directed by Nathan Fielder and Primetime filmmaker Lance Oppenheim, the movie follows the disgraced tech entrepreneur Elizabeth Holmes in the 30 days leading up to her incarceration for fraud. It holds a "Certified Fresh" 96% critics' score and a "Verified Hot" 95% audience score on the aggregator website Rotten Tomatoes, where the consensus reads, "Nathan Fielder's ironclad commitment to the bit is pushed to its limit in You Can See Everything, an inscrutable Rorschach test of Elizabeth Holmes' psyche that's as absorbing as it is haunting."

You Can See Everything was given a textbook platform release, opening in 855 theaters before expanding into more than 2,600 locations nationwide. At its widest, the film played in 3,243 theaters. In its fourth week, it saw a decrease in its theater count for the first time, losing 770 locations. The movie has shattered box-office records for documentaries with a running domestic haul of $80 million. It has a strong chance of hitting the $100 million mark, while A24 begins campaigning for a Best Picture nomination at the Oscars.

Monopoly and Scrabble Get a Scandinavian Modern Redesign

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Monopoly and Scrabble have been fixtures of family game nights for generations, but their colorful boards and familiar pieces rarely stay out once the competition ends. When the last property has been traded or the final word spelled, everything usually disappears back into a cabinet. WS Game Company is rethinking that relationship between play and display.

The new Nordic Collection gives the two classic board games a Scandinavian-inspired redesign, replacing conventional folding boards and cardboard boxes with warm wood, understated graphics, and carefully considered details. Launched last month, the collection draws on the principles of Scandinavian modernism to create games that feel as much like design objects as they do as games.

“The Nordic Collection was inspired by the rich design history of Scandinavian modern design,” explains Kerry Addis, co-owner and COO of WS Game Company. She points to designers such as Hans Wegner and Arne Jacobsen, who combined natural materials and traditional craftsmanship with modern production techniques to create objects that were both innovative and inviting. For the Nordic Collection, the goal was to bring that same balance of familiarity and innovation to the games’ form, construction, and functionality.

The goal was to create something that could fit comfortably into an existing interior while offering meaningful innovations in form, construction, and practicality.

The most immediately noticeable change is the material palette. Both Monopoly Nordic and Scrabble Nordic feature wooden game boards made from individual panels that connect through concealed magnets. Instead of unfolding a conventional board, players snap the pieces together, creating a sturdy playing surface that breaks down for compact storage.

The assembly itself was designed to be as satisfying as the finished product. “The intuitive magnetic connections allow the user to snap the panels easily together, creating a solution that is as elegant and functional as it is satisfying to use,” explains James Fuschetti, WS Game Company’s senior industrial designer. Cork backing adds warmth while helping prevent the boards from sliding during a game.

Working with manufacturing partners, the designers selected woods and stains that would produce a warm tone with a subtle luster. Generously rounded corners on the storage boxes and other components soften their appearance while making them more comfortable to handle.

The result is a collection designed to complement a range of interiors rather than compete with them. Each game comes in a coordinating beechwood box that can sit comfortably on a shelf or coffee table, with the components stored neatly inside.

Each game comes in a coordinating beechwood box that keeps the components neatly organized while allowing the games to remain on display between uses.

Beyond the wooden construction, smaller details reveal how WS Game Company balanced Scandinavian influences with the visual identities of two instantly recognizable games.

In Monopoly Nordic, the traditional houses have become miniature pine trees. Instead of placing four houses side by side on a property, players stack them vertically to create increasingly tall conifers. As the game progresses, the board begins to resemble a Scandinavian forest.

The graphic design draws on the region’s history, too. Monopoly’s Chance and Community Chest cards have been recolored light blue and red, a subtle nod to early Swedish editions of the game. The railroad icon has been reimagined as an electric trolley, while the custom money takes cues from modernist Scandinavian banknotes, pairing crisp graphics with patterns inspired by traditional folk art.

Despite these updates, the game retains its recognizable corner-square icons, Chance question mark, and classic playing tokens, including the top hat and shoe. A wooden banker’s tray, die-cast tokens, and custom property deeds round out the set.

Scrabble Nordic takes a similarly considered approach. Its scorepad features graphics inspired by Scandinavian banknotes and folk art, while wooden letter tiles and racks bring warmth to the familiar game. The set also includes a microsuede tile bag, a pencil, and a sand timer with wooden end caps.

The designers were careful to preserve the details that make Scrabble instantly recognizable, from the size and weight of the letter tiles to the small triangular markings along the edges of premium squares. Those markings help players identify bonus spaces even when tiles cover the board, preserving a small but important functional detail of the original design.

Together, the two editions demonstrate how familiar games can be updated through materials, graphics, and playful details without losing the elements that make them enjoyable in the first place.

The collection builds on an idea WS Game Company has explored for years: Board games can be part of a home’s décor rather than hidden away between uses.

Its Vintage Bookshelf Collection takes a similar approach, packaging classic games in formats intended to blend into a bookshelf. The Nordic Collection extends that thinking through natural materials, a more sculptural presentation, and an emphasis on the physical experience of playing.

“We’ve always believed that good design should have staying power,” Addis says. Rather than chasing fleeting trends, the company aims to create products that can evolve with people’s interiors and remain relevant as their lives change.

As more daily activities migrate to screens, WS Game Company sees an opportunity to bring analog experiences back into everyday life. “Board games are a natural fit, bringing people together for shared experiences,” Addis says.

The Nordic Collection makes a case for finding new possibilities in objects we already know. Monopoly and Scrabble don’t need to be reinvented to feel fresh. Sometimes, a thoughtful change in materials, a more intuitive construction, or a miniature forest of wooden houses is enough to make a familiar game worth leaving out long after everyone has gone home.

Dreeze launches with $33M to recover entire cloud applications, not just their data

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Israeli cloud application recovery startup Dreeze Inc. formally launched today with $33 million in new funding to bring what it calls Application Revival to enterprises running workloads in the public cloud.

Dreeze provides software aimed at what happens after a backup has done its job. Cloud-native applications depend on infrastructure, identities and secrets as well as data, and all of it has to come back in the right order before an application will run.

The company calls the manual work that follows a “recovery scramble.” Searching for credentials and rebuilding connections by hand can keep an application down for days or weeks after its data is restored.

Preparation starts well before an outage, when Dreeze’s software traces each cloud resource and dependency back to the application that relies on it. Its recovery plan comes from what is actually running, and changes to the environment update it automatically.

Configuration files, keys and application data go into an immutable, air-gapped vault, which customers can replicate across regions. When an outage hits, Dreeze rebuilds those pieces, plus the infrastructure and identities around them, in dependency order. Teams can also rehearse the full plan ahead of time as an automated test that ends with a working copy of the application, and production is left alone while it runs.

Getting the data back “doesn’t mean the business is back,” co-founder and Chief Executive Ziv Kedem said. With cyberattacks increasing, he said, enterprises need to plan on the basis that a critical application will go down at some point.

Jeremy Miller, founder and chief executive of Denver-based technology consulting firm Coravant LLC, said his firm wanted to work with Dreeze “from the start.” Most of its customers can back up public cloud data without much trouble, he said. Their worry is restarting a whole application with every dependency in sequence.

Dreeze is the third recovery company from Kedem and his brother Oded Kedem, its chief technology officer. The pair sold Kashya Inc. to EMC Corp. in 2006. Zerto Ltd., the disaster recovery software company they started in 2009, went to Hewlett Packard Enterprise Co. for $374 million in 2021. Avi Raichel, Dreeze’s third co-founder, became Zerto’s chief operating officer that year, shortly before the sale.

Norwest Venture Partners and Bessemer Venture Partners led the seed round. Claltech also participated. Dreeze employs 70 people across Israel and the U.S. and plans to put the money into product development and a larger sales and marketing team.

Few teams understand the recovery problem “as deeply as this one,” Norwest General Partner Dror Nahumi said. In a blog post, Bessemer said Dreeze uses artificial intelligence to work out how thousands of cloud resources connect to the applications they serve. That picture shifts with every change, including ones made by AI agents.

Dreeze’s software is generally available now for Amazon Web Services Inc. customers. A version for Microsoft Corp.’s Azure is in early access, with general availability planned for early next year.

Is It Safe to Eat Raw Cranberries?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This holiday star rarely turns up in the fruit bowl. Could you eat cranberries raw if you wanted to?

Most of us have likely eaten cranberries without ever trying one on its own. The fruit is familiar enough to be indispensable at Thanksgiving, but rarely considered when we’re in the mood for a bowl of berries to snack on.

I live near Bandon, the Cranberry Capital of Oregon, where enthusiasm for the fruit extends well beyond Thanksgiving. Cranberry bogs are part of the landscape, and local berries fill jars of jam and syrup at roadside stands. This fall, my family attended the annual cranberry festival, where a friend of ours was crowned Cranberry Queen. (It’s a small town.)

Even here, though, cranberries generally arrive at the table with enough sugar to soften their bite. That sharpness is welcome in a buttery loaf or in a cranberry sauce alongside a rich turkey dinner, but a plain berry is a very different experience. So is all that preparation a matter of preference, or do cranberries actually need to be cooked before you eat them?

Yes, cranberries are safe to eat raw. There’s no need to cook them first; simply rinse them under cool running water and discard any that are soft, shriveled or spoiled. What happens after that is largely between you and your tolerance for sour foods.

Raw cranberries are firm and sharply tart, with a bitterness that can get lost beneath the sugar in cranberry sauce and muffins. They’re also astringent, meaning they leave you with a dry, puckering feeling in your mouth. If you loved the sour fruit trend of adding citric acid to grapes, then you might happily eat a few plain cranberries, but their absence from the average person’s fruit salad is understandable.

They have plenty to offer nutritionally, too. One cup of raw cranberries provides about 4 grams of fiber and 13 milligrams of vitamin C, along with naturally occurring antioxidants. They’re also naturally low in sugar, though that tends to change once we start mixing them with the things that make us love them.

I like cranberries best when their tartness is balanced by something rich or sweet. In a cranberry orange bread, the berries become little pockets of sharp fruit that break up the sweetness, while orange zest and warm spices round out the flavor. They do the same for muffins, cakes and scones, where an acid bite is particularly welcome among all that butter and sugar.

Of course, making sauce remains a very good use for a bag of cranberries. Simmering softens the berries until they burst, and sweetening lets you appreciate their flavor without quite so much puckering. At Thanksgiving, that acidity is part of the appeal: A spoonful brings some relief to a plate largely devoted to gravy, cream and butter. I’m equally happy to see the fruit on the next day’s turkey sandwich along with plenty of mayonnaise.

If you’d rather leave the berries uncooked, chopping them finely makes their intensity easier to distribute throughout a dish. A fresh relish with orange and sugar preserves their crisp texture, while cranberry salsa takes them in a savory direction. You can also blend a small handful into a smoothie with sweeter fruit.

And if you bought more than you need, cranberries freeze beautifully. Set some aside for baking after the holidays. A cranberry-orange loaf will be just as appreciated when the oven has considerably fewer obligations.

Corey was born and raised in New Jersey. Graduated with degrees theater and literature from Ramapo College of New Jersey. After working in administrative theater for a year in New York, he started as the Weekend Editor at CinemaBlend. He's since been able to work himself up to reviews, phoners, and press junkets-- and is now able to appear on camera with some of his favorite actors... just not as he would have predicted as a kid. He's particularly proud of covering horror franchises like Scream and Halloween, as well as movie musicals like West Side Story. Favorite interviews include Steven Spielberg, Spike Lee, Jamie Lee Curtis, and more.

Seagate's new hybrid drive raises some old questions

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The FireCuda Forge revisits an old idea, with some familiar questions.

Seagate doesn’t think hybrid drives are done just yet. On Tuesday, the company announced the launch of the new FireCuda Forge line of storage drives. The new drives combine the high capacity of a traditional plattered HDD with the speed and responsiveness of a user-supplied solid-state drive (SSD). The new hybrid storage solution is Seagate’s first to be powered and managed by the new Alkemi intelligent storage platform.

The FireCuda Forge is available right now through Seagate’s web store, and comes in 8TB ($699) or 20TB ($899) HDD configurations—neither of which includes the required SSD. Users will have to bring their own SSD, with some specific restrictions. The FireCuda Forge is compatible with M.2 2280 NVMe drives that are PCIe Gen3 or higher. The maximum supported NVMe capacity is 1TB, which means that’s as much data as you’ll be able to dedicate to storage on the “fast side” of the hybrid setup. The NVMe will require minimum speeds of 2,400MB/s read and 2,000MB/s write, and a unit with DRAM cache is recommended. Seagate claims 2,500MB/s read and 2,000MB/s write speeds for the combined system, along with USB4 and support for Windows 11 and macOS 26+.

Credit: Seagate Credit: Seagate

After dipping to historical lows earlier in the decade, storage pricing per TB has exploded amid the AI boom. According to data collected by Tom’s Hardware, some solid-state drives now cost two to three times their historic lows, and it’s not just SSDs. Mechanical drives haven’t been immune either. According to PCServerParts, a Seagate Exos 24TB drive that sold for $298.99 in the summer of 2025 was priced at $699.99 by August 2026.

Hybrid drives had largely fallen out of fashion since their heyday in the early to mid 2010s. The promise then was similar to what Seagate is offering now. A large HDD is combined with a smaller SSD and handed to the user as a single storage pool. Apple introduced its own hybrid solution back in 2012 with the Mac mini’s Fusion Drive. When Ars opened up a unit, the Fusion Drive system was found to write in 128KB tiered blocks. Information was generally written to the SSD first, and was then migrated to the HDD as needed. If the files were frequently used, they might find a more permanent home on the SSD side of things.

As solid-state storage continued getting cheaper, the hybrid approach was largely abandoned. Fast-forward to our current storage cost predicament in 2026, and Seagate’s line of thinking that everything old is new again starts to hold water. All storage costs have exploded, but the 2012 math still works today. NAND flash is still fast, but expensive, and traditional mechanical drives are at least comparatively cheaper per TB for higher capacity storage.

The problem with Apple’s Fusion solution was its fragility. If one of the two drives failed, that was likely it. Recovery depended on both drives coming together as a single volume. If one of those two disks experienced a physical failure, recovery became difficult since the remaining working drive depended on the now broken drive’s data to rebuild the full system picture.

According to Seagate’s press release, the Alkemi intelligent storage platform takes a different approach. Where Apple’s Fusion Drives used tiering managed by macOS, Alkemi manages the storage pool directly from the enclosure. Seagate says, “Alkemi learns and adapts over time to keep high-demand content fast and accessible on a user’s SSD, while the HDD provides space for growing libraries. Behind the scenes, Alkemi manages data placement, giving users the simplicity of a single storage drive.” The product data sheet PDF from the FireCuda Forge website describes the Alkemi system as a two-tier storage system, but doesn’t elaborate any further on whether the system uses SSD caching, automated tiering, a combination of the two, or something else entirely.

The announcement doesn’t address how the Alkemi platform manages the data between the two drives. The old Intel Smart Response Technology kept failure risks low by mirroring data from the mechanical HDD to the faster SSD, with the HDD retaining the authoritative copy. The optional write-back mode could still hold new data on the SSD, though. Apple’s Fusion Drive instead tiered data in 128KB blocks across the storage pool, storing data on both drives. The distinction matters, and is probably why Ars asked the same question after Apple’s hybrid announcement 14 years ago.

Seagate’s announcement speaks of “intelligent data tiering” without further explanation. The data system promises that your most accessed files will be quickest to access via intelligent storage management, but stops short of explaining whether one drive going down means failure for the whole system. There’s also no mention of how the Alkemi system distributes data on first boot. Does all the new written data go directly to the SSD until the 1TB cap is crossed, and then the system starts determining which files are important enough to remain on the speedier drive? Seagate seems to be keeping things mum for the moment, but as the drives have already launched for sale, there should be answers to all of these questions coming soon.

Seagate hasn’t published technical documentation regarding how the Alkemi system handles drive failure or repair. Until it does, the new FireCuda Forge drives offer the same appeal as Apple’s old Fusion Drives: SSD speed with HDD capacity, along with the same questions Ars was asking about hybrid storage years ago.

Ars Technica has been separating the signal from the noise for over 25 years. With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Killing Floor Studio Cofounder Blames Spiraling Layoffs On ‘Woke Leftists’

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

John GibsonKilling Floor 3Maneater 2Tripwire InteractiveWoke Leftists

Tripwire Interactive, maker of Maneater and Killing Floor 3, announced fresh layoffs this week. The Georgia-based studio is letting go of 36 developers. These cuts come on top of an earlier round of restructuring in March. Some observers have blamed the downsizing on Killing Floor 3‘s rough reception on Steam. Tripwire Interactive cofounder John Gibson has blamed it on “woke leftists.”

“Killing Floor 2, a 2016 game, still had about 6,000 average daily players on Steam last month,” gaming consultant Iain Garner posted on X in response to this week’s layoff announcement. “KF 3 had about 460. KF2 sits at 88 percent positive reviews, while KF3 is at 44 percent.”

Gibson, who cofounded the studio in 2005 with Alan Wilson, quote-tweeted Garner’s post and posited a different reason for Tripwire’s struggles. “This wasn’t the issue, it was that woke leftists got me, the CEO, key leader and creative force ousted and the company forced to be sold,” he wrote. “This narrative that CEO’s are just greedy stuffed shirts is destroyed by this result. The same team that made KF3 made KF2, minus its leader.”

He continued, “I’m not saying mistakes weren’t made following my departure, and I’m not saying I was the only talented person there. I lead a great team of talented individuals, and we succeeded as a team. But pull the linchpin and the machine collapses. Like kicking Steve Jobs out of Apple.”

You might be wondering what the hell Gibson is talking about. Woke leftists? Kicking Steve Jobs out of Apple?

I'm not saying mistakes weren't made following my departure, and I'm not saying I was the only talented person there. I lead a great team of talented individuals, and we succeeded as a team. But pull the linchpin and the machine collapses. Like kicking Steve Jobs out of Apple.

— John Gibson (@RammJaeger) November 10, 2026

The game industry veteran was referencing his departure from the studio in 2021 over his support for an abortion ban that the Supreme Court upheld in Texas prior to Roe v. Wade being overturned. “Proud of #USSupremeCourt affirming the Texas law banning abortion for babies with a heartbeat,” he wrote at the time. “As an entertainer I donât get political often. Yet with so many vocal peers on the other side of this issue, I felt it was important to go on the record as a pro-life game developer.”

Tripwire Interactive’s development partners distanced themselves from the statement, as did the studio itself. “The comments given by John Gibson are of his own opinion, and do not reflect those of Tripwire Interactive as a company,” it wrote in a statement at the time. “His comments disregarded the values of our whole team, our partners and much of our broader community. Our leadership team at Tripwire are deeply sorry and are unified in our commitment to take swift action and to foster a more positive environment.”

Gibson stepped down as CEO days later, cofounder Alan Wilson took over, and Tripwire was sold to Embracer Group a year later, where it was put under Saber Interactive. Embracer began unraveling and Saber Interactive was sold off, but Tripwire remained as part of Embracer. Matthew LoPilato, who was previously the CFO, is now the CEO, and Wilson, who was the CEO, remains a VP within the company.

“Our team is focused on our internal projects, including active development of Maneater 2, ongoing work on Killing Floor 3, and publishing efforts under the Tripwire Presents label,” Tripwire announced alongside this week’s layoffs. “We are continuing to give the best support possible to all of our games and remain committed to our awesome community.”

Gibson is onto new and different things, but clearly still resentful of how everything shook out. “For clarity, they didn’t oust the old guard, just me,” he wrote in a follow-up comment on X. “They definitely destroyed the culture and it turns out a company doing that and kicking out it’s founder and leader isn’t good for business. It’s a shame so many good people got caught in the crossfire.”

GE Vernova's Backlog Hit $197 Billion and the Stock Fell Anyway. Here's What a $10,000 Investment Could Be Worth by 2030.

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

GE Vernova's backlog is large and supports predictable future revenue. However, there is a range of outcomes for its future valuation based on the sustainability of order flows through 2030.

Power, electrification, and wind turbine company GE Vernova's (GEV -3.90%) backlog recently increased to $197 billion at the end of the third quarter. Furthermore, it's now highly likely to be beyond the $200 billion figure that CEO Scott Strazik believes is a "humble milestone" on the way to long-term growth. That growth will be driven by services revenue from its rapidly growing installed base of gas turbines (power segment) and electrical equipment (electrification segment) and services sales. Here's a look at what an investment in the stock could be worth in a few years.

2030 may seem a bit far out, but the reality is GE Vernova's backlog makes its revenue relatively predictable. For reference, the midpoint of management's 2026 revenue guidance is $47 billion, or less than a quarter of the current backlog.

In the company's Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) filings, GE Vernova reported that 97% of its equipment backlog, or remaining performance obligations (RPO), of $104 billion is expected to be recognized within five years, as is 54% of its $92.6 billion in services RPO. Adding these figures together gives a figure of $150 billion by the third quarter of 2031. However, that's only part of the story, because GE Vernova will also generate revenue from new orders signed during the period in question (about a third of its equipment RPO is recognized within a year).

All told, GE Vernova's revenue is relatively predictable, and, based on the Wall Street consensus from Visible Alpha, it will generate $79.3 billion in revenue in 2030 and $13.1 billion in free cash flow (FCF), implying a price-to-FCF multiple of 14.9.

The question then turns to what GE Vernova should trade at in 2030 given its growth prospects thereafter. Based on the visibility in its backlog, a conservative estimate would assume it is a mature cash-cow type of company thereafter and place it at 20 times FCF in 2030. This implies it's 34.2% undervalued right now, assuming it's trading at 14.9 times 2030 FCF. As such, $10,000 invested now would be worth $13,420 in 2030.

That said, investors should note that each gas turbine installed generates a long-term stream of higher-margin services revenue, and the more equipment installed (notably its largest HA turbines), the longer the revenue stream. For example, Strazik believes the HA turbines currently under contract will generate $100 billion in future services revenue over the next 20 years.

As such, if GE Vernova continues to win orders at the current rate in 2030, it will trade closer to the 25x FCF it trades at in 2027, as investors price in future growth. This implies it's 67.8% undervalued, meaning a $10,000 investment will be worth $16,778.

All told, something between the $16,778 and $13,420 figures seems reasonable to me, and the good news is the company's orders tend to be very early cycle (securing power for a future data center is a critical first step), so if there really is going to be a slowdown in AI investment as a result of recent political events, then GE Vernova investors will see it first in the company's order trends and be able to adjust accordingly.
